Abelian envelopes of exact categories and highest weight categories
Agnieszka Bodzenta, Alexey Bondal

Abstract
. We define admissible and weakly admissible subcategories in exact categories and prove that the former induce semi-orthogonal decompositions on the derived categories. We develop the theory of thin exact categories, an exact-category analogue of triangulated categories generated by exceptional sequences. The right and left abelian envelopes of exact categories are introduced, an example being the category of coherent sheaves on a scheme as the right envelope of the category of vector bundles. The existence of right (left) abelian envelopes is proven for exact categories with projectively (injectively) generating subcategories with weak (co)kernels. We show that highest weight categories are precisely the right/left envelopes of thin categories. Ringel duality on highest weight categories is interpreted as a duality between the right and left abelian envelopes of a thin exact category. A duality for thin exact categories compatible with Ringel duality is introduced by means of derived categories and Serre functor on them.



August 8, 2026

1.
Introduction

This paper is a result of an attempt to understand the categorical foundations of the theory of highest weight categories. Highest weight categories, being the same as the categories of representations for quasi-hereditary algebras, emerged in papers of Cline, Parshall and Scott
[
CPS88
,
PS88
]
as a result of a formalisation of properties of categories of perverse sheaves on nicely stratified topological spaces. They attracted a lot of attention in Representation Theory because categories of representations of various origin turned out to be highest weight,
cf.

[
PS88
,
Don87
,
Rin91
,
DR92
,
BS18
]
.

By definition, highest weight categories are abelian categories possessing a set of objects, called
standard objects
, which have some good properties, which imply, in particular, that they form a full exceptional sequence
[
Bon89
]
in the derived category
[
CPS88
]
.

Our point of view in this paper, inspired by the work of Claus Michael Ringel, is that to understand highest weight categories one should shift the attention from abelian to exact categories. Studying the properties of the relevant exact categories lead us to the notion of a
thin
exact category.

We consider an exact category counterpart of the notion of admissible subcategory in a triangulated category
[
Bon89
]
. It happens that there are two levels of admissibility for subcategories in exact categories, which we call
weak admissibility
and just
admissibility
. Thin category is an exact category that has a filtration by admissible subcategories, such that graded factors are categories of vectors spaces. One can think of it as an exact category endowed with a suitable replacement of what-would-be a full exceptional sequence, but for exact categories. This gadget has already been discussed by V. Dlab and C. Ringel in the abelian category set-up under the name of
standarisable collection

[
DR92
]
.

To match the definition in the triangulated world, we show that the derived category of an admissible subcategory in an exact category is an admissible subcategory (in the ‘triangulated sense’) of the derived category of the ambient category.

The main innovative tool of this paper are abelian envelopes of exact categories. We distinguish the right
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
and left
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
envelopes of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. One known way to associate an abelian category to an exact category is via the
exact abelian hull
. It is the universal abelian category among those which allow an exact functor from
{\mathcal{E}}
into them. The
left and right envelopes
are similar universal abelian categories but for functors which are only right/left exact. The existence of the hull was proven in
[
Ade73
,
Ste14
]
. An arbitrary exact category does not need to have the right abelian envelope (see Example
4.7
). We prove the existence of the right/left envelope under the condition that the exact category has enough projectives/injectives and the full subcategory of projectives/injectives has weak kernels/cokernels. We discuss further instances when the envelopes exist. For example, we show that the
right envelope of the category of vector bundles on a good enough scheme is the category of coherent sheaves.
Under suitable finiteness conditions we prove the derived
equivalence of an exact category with its envelopes.

We show that highest weight categories are precisely the right/left envelopes of thin exact categories. The thin category related to the highest weight category is the extension closure of the set of (co)standard objects in it.
The envelopes of thin categories possess canonical strict (co)localising filtrations which are relevant to the partial order on simple objects in the highest weight categories. We give a characterisation of the thin subcategory inside its envelope via adjunction morphisms induced by the filtration.

Ringel duality (originally for quasi-hereditary algebras) acquires in our context a natural interpretation as a transfer from the right to the left envelope and vice versa. Also it leads to a duality (which we call also Ringel duality) on thin categories, where the dual of thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
is constructed as the intersection inside derived category
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
with
the image of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
under the inverse of the Serre functor.

Structure of the paper and its results in detail.

We begin by recalling the notion of an exact category (see Section
2.1
).
The main (and the only) source of examples of exact categories are full subcategories
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
of abelian categories closed under extensions. Then short exact sequences in
{\mathcal{A}}
whose all terms lie in
{\mathcal{E}}
give an exact structure on
{\mathcal{E}}
.

We study the structure of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
by means of (perpendicular) torsion pairs.
Full subcategories
{\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
form a
torsion pair
if
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})=0
and any object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
fits into a conflation
T\to E\to F
with
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Such conflation is functorial in
E
, see Section
2.2
. The data of a torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is equivalent to a
weakly right admissible
subcategory
\iota_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
(i.e.
\iota_{*}
admits the right adjoint
\iota^{!}
and the adjunction counit
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\to E
is an inflation, for any
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
), similarly for a
weakly left admissible
subcategory
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
, see Proposition
2.3
.

Torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is
perpendicular
if further
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})=0
. We define a
right (left) admissible subcategory
as a torsion class
{\mathcal{T}}
(torsion-free class
{\mathcal{F}}
) in a perpendicular torsion pair.
By Theorem
2.11
, a torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is perpendicular if and only if one of functors
\iota^{!}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
,
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
is exact, hence both.

For a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
, we introduce the subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-projective objects
. We prove that it is a square zero extension of
{\mathcal{F}}
in the case when
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq k\textrm{--vect}
, see Theorem
2.15
.

The bounded derived category
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
is well-behaved for
weakly idempotent split
exact categories
(see Section
2.7
).
An exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
with a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is weakly idempotent split if and only if
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
are (see Proposition
2.24
).
If this is the case, a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
on
{\mathcal{E}}
yields a semi-orthogonal decomposition
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})=\langle{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}})\rangle
, see Theorem
2.25
.
The change of order of
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
appears here just to match the traditional conventions.

We define a
strict admissible left/right filtration
on an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
(see Section
2.6
). It is a counterpart in exact categories of a longer semi-orthogonal decomposition and it induces such on
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
, see Theorem
2.26
.

In Section
3
we introduce a class of exact categories which is our main attraction throughout the paper.
A
thin exact category
is a
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits
and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
-finite
k
-linear exact category which admits a right admissible filtration
0={\mathcal{T}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\subset\ldots\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{n}={\mathcal{E}}
with graded factors
equivalent to
k\textrm{--vect}
.
The derived category of a thin exact category admits a full exceptional sequence
[
Bon89
]
, see Proposition
3.4
. Hence,
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
admits the
Serre functor

\mathbb{S}

[
BK89
]
.

Using the theory of universal extensions developed in Section
2.5
, we prove that any thin exact category admits a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
, see Proposition
3.8
. This is a categorical version of the construction of tilting objects for standarizable collections by V. Dlab and C. Ringel
[
DR92
]
.

We endow
\Lambda
, the set of irreducible objects in a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
, with a canonical partial order, see Section
3.1
. Principal lower ideals in this poset allow us to assign a right admissible subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
to any
\lambda\in\Lambda
, see Proposition
3.5
.

In Section
4
we introduce the main tool of our paper, the machinery of
abelian envelopes
. The
right abelian envelope
for an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
together with a right exact functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
which is universal for right exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
, with
{\mathcal{A}}
abelian. The
left abelian envelope

{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is defined analogously. Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
, see (
25
), we mostly restrict our attention to right abelian envelopes.

If the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{E}}
exists then it is equivalent to the subcategory of compact objects in
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
and the functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is faithful (see Lemma
4.5
). We don’t know whether it is always fully faithful, but when it is so,
{\mathcal{E}}
is a fully exact subcategory of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
(see Lemma
4.8
).

The right abelian envelope extends to a monad on the 2-category of exact categories admitting the right abelian envelopes, see Section
4.3
.
Algebras over this monad are identified with abelian categories, see Proposition
4.10
.

Using a statement of
[
KS06
]
, we prove that an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
is the right abelian envelope of a full subcategory
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
closed under extensions and kernels of epimorphisms, provided every object of
{\mathcal{A}}
is a quotient of an object of
{\mathcal{E}}
, see Theorem
4.11
.
This implies that the right envelope of the category of vector bundles is the category of coherent sheaves (see Corollary
4.12
).

If an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
admits a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
with weak kernels, then the right abelian envelope
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is equivalent to the category
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
of finitely presented functors
{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\to\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits
.
Moreover,
{\mathcal{E}}\subset\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
is a fully exact subcategory,
see Theorem
4.19
.
In particular, a thin category admits the right and the left envelope, see Corollary
4.22
.

Under the same conditions we prove that
{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
, Proposition
4.24
.
If further
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
has finite global dimension then
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
, see Theorem
4.25
.

A perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
on an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
induces a colocalising subcategory of the right envelope
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
if
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
have right envelopes, see Proposition
4.28
.
More generally, a strict admissible filtration on
{\mathcal{E}}
with graded factors admitting right envelopes induces a colocalising filtration on
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, see Theorem
4.35
.

In Section
5
we focus on highest weight categories and show that they are abelian envelopes of thin categories.

Given a highest weight category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
, its subcategories
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
, of objects with filtration with
standard, respectively costandard, factors, are thin. This gives an essentially one-to-one correspondence between highest weight abelian categories and thin exact categories, the inverse operation given by taking the right or left envelope (see Propositions
5.6
,
5.7
and Theorem
5.8
).

An abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
might have several highest weight structures which depend on the choice of partial orders on the set of isomorphism classes of simple objects. We consider the equivalence relation on the partial orders defined by equality of the corresponding thin subcategories
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta)\subset{\mathcal{A}}
, see (
30
). We prove that posets
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{\prime}
are equivalent if and only if
\Lambda^{\prime}
dominates the canonical poset of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
, see Proposition
5.14
.

We characterise a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
inside
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
using the strict filtration on
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
induced by the canonical filtration on
{\mathcal{E}}
(see Theorem
4.35
).
Objects of
{\mathcal{E}}

are precisely those
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
for which the adjunction counits
{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda}^{*}A\to A
are monomorphisms, where
{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda})\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is the functor induced by the inclusion
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
,
see Theorem
5.16
.

A highest weight category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
admits
Ringel dual

\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})

[
Rin91
]
, originally defined as the category of modules over the endomorphism algebra of the characteristic tilting object
T\in{\mathcal{A}}
. In Section
6.1
we prove
(see Theorem
6.3
)

\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda}))\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})).

Since
{\mathcal{A}}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda}))
, Ringel duality is the transfer from the right to the left abelian envelope of a thin category.

We show that a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
is the intersection of its left and right envelopes in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
, see Theorem
6.5
.
We define the
Ringel dual

\mathbf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}})
of a thin exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
,

\mathbf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}):={\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})\cap\mathbb{S}^{-1}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}).

The category
\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}})
is again thin exact and
\bf{RDT}(\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}))
is canonically equivalent to
{\mathcal{E}}
, see Proposition
6.7
.
Given a highest weight category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
, the thin exact categories
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
are Ringel dual to each other.

In Appendix
A
we show that the data of an abelian recollement is equivalent to a
bi-localising subcategory (see Proposition
A.5
). We establish a one-to-one correspondence between Serre subcategories in a finite length abelian category and subsets of the set of simple objects in it (this should be well-known to experts), see Lemma
A.8
.
For Deligne finite categories (i.e. finite length categories which admit projective generators), we prove that bi-localising subcategories are exactly Serre subcategories, see Proposition
A.9
.

Notation
For a field
k
we denote by
k\textrm{-vect}
the category of finite dimensional
k
-vector spaces. To simplify notation we denote by
\otimes
the tensor product over the ground field
k
.

2.
Exact categories

We recall the definition of an exact category and introduce the crucial notion of left and right admissible subcategories. We characterise them in terms of exactness of appropriate adjoint functors. Then, we discuss strict admissible filtrations on exact categories and the induced filtrations on the derived categories.

2.1.
Preliminaries

Exact categories were defined by N. Yoneda
[
Yon60
]
, A.Heller
[
Hel58
]
and D.Quillen in
[
Qui73
]
. Below we reproduce a version of the definition as in
[
Kel90
]
.

An
exact category
is an additive category
{\mathcal{E}}
together with a fixed class
{\mathcal{S}}
of
conflations
, i.e. pairs of composable morphisms

(1)

X\xrightarrow{i}Y\xrightarrow{d}Z

such that
i
is the kernel of
d
and
d
is the cokernel of
i
. We shall say that
i
is an
inflation
and
d
a
deflation
. The class
{\mathcal{S}}
is closed under isomorphisms and the pair
({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is to satisfy the following axioms:

(Ex 0)

0\to X\xrightarrow{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{X}}X
is a conflation,

(Ex 1)

the composite of two deflations is a deflation,

(Ex 2)

the pullback of a deflation against an arbitrary morphism exists and is a deflation,

(Ex 2’)

the pushout of an inflation along an arbitrary morphism exists and is an inflation.

For objects
E
and
F
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
, we denote by
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(E,F)
the abelian group whose elements are classes of conflations

F\to X\to E
considered up to isomorphisms which are identical on
F
and
E
.

An additive functor between exact categories is
exact
if the image of every conflation is a conflation. We denote by
\mathop{\textrm{Ex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
the category whose objects are exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
and morphisms are natural transformations.

A full subcategory
{\mathcal{X}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
of an exact category is said to be
closed under extensions
if for any conflation (
1
) with
X,Z\in{\mathcal{X}}
object
Y
also belongs to
{\mathcal{X}}
. If
{\mathcal{X}}
satisfies this condition then the conflations in
{\mathcal{E}}
whose all three terms lie in
{\mathcal{X}}
yield an exact structure on
{\mathcal{X}}
. By
fully exact subcategory of

{\mathcal{E}}
we mean a full subcategory closed under extensions and endowed with the induced exact structure. The embedding functor of a fully exact subcategory is clearly exact and reflects exactness.

An additive category
{\mathcal{B}}
has the
split exact structure
with deflations split epimorphisms and inflations split monomorphisms.

An abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
is regarded as an exact category
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{S}})
with the class
{\mathcal{S}}
of conflations being all short exact sequences in
{\mathcal{A}}
. In particular exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
from an exact category to an abelian category make sense.

A rich source of examples of exact categories is given by extension closed subcategories of abelian categories with the induced exact structure. According to the Gabriel-Quillen theorem
[
Gab62
, II.3]
,
[
Qui73
, §2]
,
cf.

[
Lau83
, Theorem 1.0.3]
,
[
Fre64
, Ex. 4.7.3.G]
, every exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is a fully exact subcategory of an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Under this embedding
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
into an abelian category,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E_{1},E_{2})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(E_{1},E_{2})
and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E_{1},E_{2})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(E_{1},E_{2})
, for any
E_{1},E_{2}\in{\mathcal{E}}
. It follows that for any conflation (
1
) and any
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
we have the six-terms exact sequences:

(2)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,X)\to

\displaystyle\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,Y)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,Z)\to

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,X)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,Y)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,Z),

\displaystyle 0\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(Z,E)\to

\displaystyle\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(Y,E)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(X,E)\to

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(Z,E)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(Y,E)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(X,E)

2.2.
Weakly left and right admissible subcategories

Let
{\mathcal{X}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a subcategory of an additive category
{\mathcal{E}}
. We define
the right
(respectively
left
)
orthogonal subcategory

{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp_{0}}
(respectively
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{X}}
) by:

(3)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}^{\perp_{0}}=\{E\in{\mathcal{E}}\,|\,\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{X}},E)=0\},

\displaystyle{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{X}}=\{E\in{\mathcal{E}}\,|\,\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E,{\mathcal{X}})=0\}.

Category
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{X}}
is closed under quotients, i.e. given an epimorphism
X\to X^{\prime}
with
X\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp_{0}}
object
X^{\prime}
lies in
{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp_{0}}
. Similarly,
{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp_{0}}
is closed under subobjects.

Definition 2.1
.

We say that a fully exact subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is
weakly right admissible
if the inclusion functor
i_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
admits right adjoint
i^{!}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
and the adjunction counit
\varepsilon_{E}\colon i_{*}i^{!}E\to E
is an inflation, for any
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
.

Definition 2.2
.

We say that a fully exact subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is
weakly left admissible
if the inclusion functor
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
admits a left adjoint
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
and the adjunction unit
\eta_{E}\colon E\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
is a deflation, for any
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}
be fully exact subcategories of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. We say that
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a
torsion pair
if
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})=0
and any object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
fits into a conflation
T\to E\to F
with
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proposition 2.3
.

A fully exact subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly right admissible if and only if
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
, where
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}
, is a torsion pair in
{\mathcal{E}}
. In this case, every conflation
T\to E\to F
with
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
is canonically isomorphic (identically on
E
) to the functorial conflation

(4)

\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\to E\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E,

where
\iota_{*}:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
, and
\delta_{*}:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
are the embedding functors,
\iota^{!}
and
\delta^{*}
are their adjoints and morphisms in (
4
) are, respectively, the adjunction counit and unit. An analogous fact holds for weakly left admissible subcategories.

Proof.

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a torsion pair in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Consider a conflation
T\xrightarrow{in}E\xrightarrow{d}F
with
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
.

For
T^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
, the map
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(T^{\prime},T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(T^{\prime},E)
given by the composition with
in
is an isomorphism, since its cokernel belongs to
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(T^{\prime},F)=0
. Similarly,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(F,F^{\prime})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E,F^{\prime})
, for any
F^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Hence, we have canonical isomorphisms
T\simeq\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E
and
F\simeq\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
. Under these isomorphisms, the adjunction counit
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\to E
is identified with
in
, hence an inflation, and the adjunction unit
E\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
is identified with
d
, hence a deflation. Thus,
{\mathcal{T}}
is weakly right admissible and
{\mathcal{F}}
is weakly left admissible.

Let now
\iota_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
be a weakly right admissible fully exact subcategory. We aim to show that
F
defined as the cokernel of
T:=\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\to E
belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}
. For
T^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
the first map in the exact sequence
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T^{\prime},T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T^{\prime},E)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T^{\prime},F)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T^{\prime},T)
(see (
2
)) is an isomorphism. Hence, in order to show that
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(T^{\prime},F)=0
it suffices to check that for any
\varphi\colon T^{\prime}\to F
the induced extension of
T^{\prime}
by
T
splits:

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{in}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{in}}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{T^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}

Object
T^{\prime\prime}
belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}
, because
{\mathcal{T}}
is closed under extensions. Since
T=\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E
morphism
\overline{\varphi}
factors via
\psi
, i.e.
\overline{\varphi}=in\circ\psi
. Then
in\circ\psi\circ\overline{in}=\overline{\varphi}\circ\overline{in}=in
which implies that
\psi\circ\overline{in}=\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits
, as
in
is a monomorphism. Hence the induced extension of
T^{\prime}
by
T
splits and
\varphi=0
. As
\varphi
was arbitrary,
F\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}
, i.e.
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}})
is a torsion pair.

We have shown that torsion pairs are in one to one correspondence with weakly right admissible subcategories. By considering the opposite categories we obtain an analogous result for weakly left admissible subcategories.
∎

Note that functors
\iota^{!}
and
\delta^{*}
are not necessarily exact. In the next subsection, we put stronger conditions on the subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
that ensure exactness of these functors.

Proposition 2.4
.

We have an involutive duality between weakly right admissible subcategories
{\mathcal{T}}
and weakly left admissible subcategories
{\mathcal{F}}
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
:

(5)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}:={}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}.

In particular,
{\mathcal{T}}
is closed under quotients in
{\mathcal{E}}
, and
{\mathcal{F}}
is closed under subobjects in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a weakly right admissible subcategory and
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}
. Proposition
2.3
implies that
{\mathcal{E}}=({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a torsion pair and
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly left admissible. The decomposition (
4
) of any
T^{\prime}\in{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}
with respect to the torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a conflation
T\to T^{\prime}\to 0
, with
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
. Hence
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}={}^{\perp_{0}}({\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}})\subset{\mathcal{T}}
. As the inverse inclusion is clear, we have:
{\mathcal{T}}={}^{\perp_{0}}({\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}})
.

By transferring to the opposite categories, we conclude that if
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly left admissible then
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}
is weakly right admissible and
{\mathcal{F}}=(^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}})^{\perp_{0}}
.
∎

2.3.
Left and right admissible subcategories

Let
{\mathcal{X}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a subcategory of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. We define
the right
(respectively
left
),
perpendicular subcategory

{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp}
(respectively
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{X}}
) by:

(6)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}^{\perp}=\{E\in{\mathcal{E}}\,|\,\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{X}},E)=0=\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}({\mathcal{X}},E)\},

\displaystyle{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{X}}=\{E\in{\mathcal{E}}\,|\,\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E,{\mathcal{X}})=0=\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(E,{\mathcal{X}})\}.

Category
{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp}
is closed under kernels of deflations, i.e. given a conflation
E^{\prime}\to E\to E^{\prime\prime}
with
E,E^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp}
object
E^{\prime}
lies in
{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp}
. Similarly,
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{X}}
is closed under cokernels of inflations.

Perpendicular categories in
abelian
categories were introduced by W. Geigle and H. Lenzing
[
GL91
]
, who emphasized their importance in the representation theory of associative algebras. In this paper, we use a more general notion of perpendicular categories in exact categories as a basic tool for studying exact categories.

Definition 2.5
.

We say that a fully exact subcategory
{\mathcal{X}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is
right
(respectively,
left
)
admissible
if
{\mathcal{E}}
admits a torsion pair
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp})
, (respectively
({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{X}})
).

In view of Proposition
2.3
,
{\mathcal{X}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is right (respectively, left) admissible if and only if
{\mathcal{X}}
is weakly right (respectively, left) admissibe and
{\mathcal{X}}^{\perp}={\mathcal{X}}^{\perp_{0}}
(respectively,
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{X}}={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{X}}
).
In particular, by Proposition
2.4
, any right admissible subcategory is closed under quotients, and any left admissible subcategory is closed under subobjects.

Proposition 2.6
.

We have an involutive duality between right admissible subcategories
{\mathcal{T}}
and left admissible subcategories
{\mathcal{F}}
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
:

(7)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}:={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a right admissible subcategory and
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}
. By Proposition
2.4
,
{\mathcal{T}}={}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}
. By our assumptions,
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
. Since
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}\subset{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{T}}
, we have the equality:
{\mathcal{T}}={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
, and
{\mathcal{F}}
is left admissible. Similarly for a left admissible subcategories
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
, we have
{\mathcal{F}}=({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}})^{\perp}
.
∎

We say that a
torsion pair

({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is
perpendicular
if
{\mathcal{T}}={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
(equivalently,
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
).

By
filtration on an object

E\in{\mathcal{E}}
we mean a sequence of inflations
0=E_{0}\to E_{1}\to\dots\to E_{n}=E
.
Since the composition of inflations is an inflation, we have an induced conflation
E_{i}\to E\to Q_{i+1}
, for every
i
. Objects
Q_{i}
fit into a sequence of deflations
E=Q_{1}\to\dots\to Q_{n}\to Q_{n+1}=0
, which equivalently define the filtration on
E
.

The
graded factors

G_{i}

of the filtration
, are defined via conflations
E_{i-1}\to E_{i}\to G_{i}
. Also they fit conflations
G_{i}\to Q_{i}\to Q_{i+1}
. All this can be deduced from the similar facts for abelian categories via the Gabriel-Quillen embedding theorem or proven directly.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a fully exact subcategory and
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
a fully exact subcategory. If every object in
{\mathcal{E}}
has a filtration with all graded factors being either in
{\mathcal{T}}
or in
{\mathcal{F}}
, then
{\mathcal{T}}
is right admissible and
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on the length of the filtration on a given object
E
that
E
fits into a conflation
T\to E\to F
with
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Such a conflation for any object in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
implies that it is in
{\mathcal{F}}
, hence
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
.

Since the quotient
Q_{2}
has a shorter filtration, it fits, by induction hypothesis, into a conflation
T_{2}\to Q_{2}\to F_{2}
, with
T_{2}\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F_{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}
. If
E_{1}\in{\mathcal{T}}
, then the pullback of
E_{1}\to E\to Q_{2}
along
T_{2}\to Q_{2}
is a conflation
\sigma:E_{1}\to E^{\prime}\to T_{2}
. Hence
E^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and
E^{\prime}\to E\to F_{2}
is the sought conflation.

If
E_{1}\in{\mathcal{F}}
, then applying the second of sequences (
2
) to the conflation
T_{2}\to Q_{2}\to F_{2}
and object
E_{1}
gives an isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(F_{2},E_{1})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q_{2},E_{1})
. This means that our conflation
E_{1}\to E\to Q_{2}
is the pullback of a conflation
E_{1}\to F\to F_{2}
along
Q_{2}\to F_{2}
:

\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{2}}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{T_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

It follows that
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
and the middle column is the sought conflation.
∎

2.4.
Exactness of adjoints for embeddings of admissible subcategories

The following property of morphisms in an exact category was proved by Yoneda in
[
Yon60
]
, then reintroduced by D. Quillen as a part of axiomatics of exact categories and later again reproved by B. Keller.

Lemma 2.8

(Quillen’s obscure axiom)
.

[
Yon60
]

[
Kel90
]

Let
d\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
which admits a kernel. If there exists
\varphi\colon X^{\prime}\to X
such that
d\circ\varphi

is a deflation then
d
is a deflation.

We will use the following lemma in the proof of Theorem
2.11
on exactness of adjoints.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
be a left admissible subcategory and
\sigma
a conflation
F\xrightarrow{in}E\xrightarrow{d}E^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{E}}
with
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Then
\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation in
{\mathcal{F}}
and
\sigma
is the pull-back of
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\sigma)
along the adjunction unit
\eta_{E^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since
\delta_{*}
reflects exactness,
\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation in
{\mathcal{F}}
if and only if
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation in
{\mathcal{E}}
. It is enough to check that
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(in)
is the kernel of
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)
. Indeed, as
\eta_{E^{\prime}}
is a deflation by Definition
2.2
, the composite
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)\circ\eta_{E}=\eta_{E^{\prime}}\circ d
is the result of composition of deflations, hence a deflation. By Quillen’s obscure axiom, Lemma
2.8
, if
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)
has a kernel, it is a deflation. Then,
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}F\xrightarrow{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(in)}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E\xrightarrow{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\prime}
is a conflation.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}:={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
and let
i^{!}
be the right adjoint to the inclusion
\iota_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
. Objects
E
and
E^{\prime}
admit the decompositions (
4
) with respect to the torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
, which occur as rows in the following diagram:

(8)

\textstyle{\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{E^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{E^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}\iota^{!}(d)}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{E}}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{in}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(in)}

We will show that
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}(d)\colon\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\to\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

Let
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
be any object. In the exact sequence
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T,F)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T,E)\xrightarrow{d\circ(-)}\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T,E^{\prime})\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T,F)
(see (
2
)), the first and the last group vanish because
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a perpendicular torsion pair. In other words,
d
induces an isomorphism of functors on
{\mathcal{T}}
:
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(-,E)\xrightarrow{d\circ(-)}\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(-,E^{\prime})
. Since
\iota^{!}E
and
\iota^{!}E^{\prime}
are objects representing these functors,
\iota^{!}(d)
is an isomorphism. Hence, so is
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}(d)
.

Since
\eta_{E}
and
\eta_{E^{\prime}}
have isomorphic kernels, the standard diagram chasing shows that
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)
has the kernel isomorphic to that of
d
. As
\eta_{F}
is an isomorphism, the kernel of
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)
is identified with
\delta^{*}\delta_{*}(in)
.

Let
\tilde{E}
be the fibre product of
E^{\prime}
and
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
over
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\prime}
. The canonical map
E\to\tilde{E}
extends to a morphism of conflation
\sigma
to the pull-back conflation
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}F\to\tilde{E}\to E^{\prime}
,
which has isomorphisms on the ends of conflations:
F\xrightarrow{\simeq}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}F
and
E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rm{id}_{E^{\prime}}}E^{\prime}
. Hence the conflations are canonically isomorphic.
∎

Lemma 2.10
.

[
Büh10
, Proposition 2.15]

The pullback of an inflation along a deflation exists and is an inflation. The pushout of a deflation along an inflation exists and is a deflation.

Theorem 2.11
.

A weakly left admissible subcategory
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is left admissible if and only if
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
is exact.
A weakly right admissible subcategory
\iota_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is right admissible if and only if
\iota^{!}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
is exact.

Proof.

Since the transfer to the opposite categories
{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
,
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
exchanges the left and right (weakly) admissible subcategories and preserves the exactness of functors, it suffices to prove the equivalence for weakly left admissible subcategories.

Consider a weakly left admissible subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
with an exact functor
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
\sigma
be a conflation
F\xrightarrow{in}E\xrightarrow{d}T
in
{\mathcal{E}}
with
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
and
T\in{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}
. Vanishing of
\delta^{*}T
and exactness of
\delta^{*}
imply that
j=\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation
\delta^{*}F\xrightarrow{\simeq}\delta^{*}E\to 0
, meaning that
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(in)
is an isomorphism
F=\delta_{*}\delta^{*}F\xrightarrow{\simeq}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
. Then
\eta_{E}\colon E\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E\simeq F
yields a splitting of
\sigma
. Hence
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}({}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}})=0
, which implies that
{\mathcal{F}}
is left admissible in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Let now
{\mathcal{F}}
be a left admissible subcategory and
\sigma
a conflation
A\xrightarrow{in}B\xrightarrow{d}C
in
{\mathcal{E}}
. We aim at showing that
\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation in
{\mathcal{F}}
. As
{\mathcal{F}}
is a fully exact subcategory, it suffices to check that
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Let
T_{A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{A}}A\xrightarrow{\eta_{A}}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A
be the decomposition (
4
) for
A
with respect to the torsion pair
({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}})
. The pushout of
\sigma
along the adjunction unit
\eta_{A}\colon A\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A
yields an extension of
C
by
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A
:

(9)

\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\widetilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{C}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{A}}
\scriptstyle{in}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{T_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{A}}
\textstyle{T_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{in\circ\varepsilon_{A}}

By Lemma
2.10
,
\gamma
is a deflation, hence the middle column in the above diagram

T_{A}\xrightarrow{in\circ\varepsilon_{A}}B\xrightarrow{\gamma}\widetilde{B}

is a conflation in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Lemma
2.9
applied to the conflation
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\xrightarrow{\alpha}\widetilde{B}\xrightarrow{\beta}C
yields a conflation
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\xrightarrow{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\alpha)}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}\xrightarrow{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\beta)}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}C
:

(10)

\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\alpha)}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\beta)}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}C}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\widetilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\widetilde{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{C}}
\textstyle{T_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\widetilde{B}}}
\textstyle{T_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{C}}

Moreover, we can apply Lemma
2.9
to the middle horizontal conflation in (
10
) and conclude that
\eta_{\widetilde{B}}
is a pullback of
\eta_{C}
, hence it is a deflation. Since
T_{C}\in{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
and
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}\in{\mathcal{F}}
, the conflation
T_{C}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\widetilde{B}}}\widetilde{B}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\widetilde{B}}}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}
is the decomposition (
4
) of
\widetilde{B}
with respect to the torsion pair
({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}})
(hence the notation for the morphisms).

Note that commutativity of (
9
) implies that
\delta^{*}(\alpha)=\delta^{*}(\gamma)\circ\delta^{*}(in)
and
\delta^{*}(d)=\delta^{*}(\beta)\circ\delta^{*}(\gamma)
. To prove that
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\sigma)
is a conflation it remains to show that
\delta^{*}(\gamma)\colon\delta^{*}B\to\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}
is an isomorphism. Indeed, by applying functor
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}
to (
9
), we see that the conflation in the top row of (
10
) is then isomorphic to
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\sigma)
:

\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\alpha)}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\beta)}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}C}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(in)}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}(d)}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}

Consider the pullback of the conflation in the middle column in (
9
) along
\varepsilon_{\widetilde{B}}\colon T_{C}\to\widetilde{B}
:

(11)

\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}}
\textstyle{T_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{in\circ\varepsilon_{A}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{\widetilde{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\widetilde{B}}}
\textstyle{T_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{\widetilde{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\textstyle{T_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\widetilde{B}}}

By Lemma
2.10
,
\zeta
is an inflation, hence
\widetilde{T}\xrightarrow{\zeta}B\xrightarrow{\theta}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}

is a conflation. Since
\widetilde{T}
is an extension of
T_{C}
by
T_{A}
, it is an object of
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
. Hence, the above conflation is of the form (
4
), and we have canonical isomorphisms
\widetilde{T}\simeq\iota_{*}\iota^{!}B
,
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\widetilde{B}\simeq\delta_{*}\delta^{*}B
, and
\theta=\eta_{B}
. By applying
\delta^{*}
to (
11
), we see that
\delta^{*}(\gamma)
is an isomorphism, which finishes the proof.
∎

Now we describe admissible subcategories in the case
{\mathcal{E}}
is an abelian category. We recall the notion of a (co)localising subcategory of an abelian category in Appendix
A
.

Proposition 2.12
.

A fully exact subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{F}}
) of an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
is right (left) admissible if and only if
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
) is a colocalising (localising) subcategory and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
(resp.
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}
).

Proof.

Assume that
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a perpendicular torsion pair in
{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
0\to A^{\prime}\to A\to A^{\prime\prime}\to 0
be a short exact sequence in
{\mathcal{A}}
. Since
\delta^{*}
is exact,
\delta^{*}(A)=0
if and only if
\delta^{*}(A^{\prime})=0
and
\delta^{*}(A^{\prime\prime})=0
. Hence,
{\mathcal{T}}
is a Serre subcategory.
Since the torsion pair is perpendicular,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}={\mathcal{F}}
. In particular,
{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{T}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}={\mathcal{F}}

[
Gab62
]
. As the embedding
{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is right adjoint to the quotient
{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{F}}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{T}}
, category
{\mathcal{T}}
is colocalising.

Now let
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
be a colocalising subcategory such that
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
. The quotient functor
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{T}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}={\mathcal{F}}
has a fully faithful right adjoint
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
. The subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is closed under extensions and subobjects.
As the inclusion functor
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
has left adjoint
\delta^{*}
,
{\mathcal{A}}
admits a torsion pair
({}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}})

[
BR07
, Proposition 1.2]
. Let
X
be an object of
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}
. Vanishing of
\delta^{*}(X)
implies that
X\in{\mathcal{T}}
, hence
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
. The inverse inclusion is clear, i.e.
{\mathcal{A}}
admits a torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
. As
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp_{0}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
the torsion pair is perpendicular and
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is right admissible.
∎

2.5.
The category of universal extensions as a square-zero extension category

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be an additive category. An
{\mathcal{A}}

bimodule
is an additive bifunctor
{\mathcal{M}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\times{\mathcal{A}}\to\textrm{Ab}
.

Let
q\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be a functor. A
lifting
of an object
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
along
q
is an object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
together with an isomorphism
\zeta\colon q(A)\xrightarrow{\simeq}B
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
be additive categories and
q\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
an additive functor. We define the
kernel bimodule

{\mathcal{K}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\times{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}b
of
q
via:

{\mathcal{K}}(A,A^{\prime})=\{\varphi\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(A,A^{\prime})\,|\,q(\varphi)=0\}.

Definition 2.13
.

We say that an additive functor
q\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
of additive categories is a
square-zero extension
of
{\mathcal{B}}
if
q
is essentially surjective, conservative and full, and the composition of any two morphisms in the kernel
{\mathcal{K}}
of
q
is zero.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
q\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be a square-zero extension with kernel
{\mathcal{K}}
. There is unique up to unique isomorphism
{\mathcal{B}}
bimodule
{\mathcal{M}}
such that
{\mathcal{K}}\simeq{\mathcal{M}}\circ(q^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\times q)
.

Proof.

Let
B_{1},B_{2}
be objects of
{\mathcal{B}}
and
(A_{2},\xi)
a lifting of
B_{2}
. We check that for two liftings
(A_{1},\zeta)
,
(A^{\prime}_{1},\zeta^{\prime})
of
B_{1}
the groups
{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})
and
{\mathcal{K}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{2})
are canonically isomorphic.

Consider the composite
q(A^{\prime}_{1})\xrightarrow{\zeta^{\prime}}B_{1}\xrightarrow{\zeta^{-1}}q(A_{1})
. Since
q
is full, there exists
\tau\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1})
such that
q(\tau)=\zeta^{-1}\circ\zeta^{\prime}
. As
q
is conservative,
\tau
is an isomorphism. Morphism
\tau
induces a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{K}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{B}}}(q(A^{\prime}_{1}),q(A_{2}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(A_{1},A_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{(-)\circ\tau}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{B}}}(q(A_{1}),q(A_{2}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\circ\zeta^{-1}\circ\zeta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{0}

Let
\tau^{\prime}\colon A^{\prime}_{1}\to A_{1}
be another morphism such that
q(\tau^{\prime})=\zeta^{-1}\circ\zeta^{\prime}
. Then
\tau-\tau^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1})
. As the composition of any two morphism in
{\mathcal{K}}
is zero we have
\varphi\circ(\tau-\tau^{\prime})=0
, for any
\varphi\in{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})
, i.e. the isomorphism
\mu\colon{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{K}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{2})
does not depend on the choice of
\tau
. Similarly for two liftings
(A_{2},\xi)
,
(A^{\prime}_{2},\xi^{\prime})
for
B_{2}
one constructs a canonical isomorphism
\nu\colon{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})\to{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A^{\prime}_{2})
. Given
f_{0}\colon B_{0}\to B_{1}
,
f_{2}\colon B_{2}\to B_{3}
one can also check that the homomorphism
{\mathcal{K}}(g_{0}\times g_{2})\colon{\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})\to{\mathcal{K}}(A_{0},A_{3})
is independent of the choice of
g_{0}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(A_{0},A_{1})
,
g_{2}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(A_{2},A_{3})
such that
q(g_{0})=f_{0}
,
q(g_{2})=f_{2}
.

Therefore
{\mathcal{M}}
defined by
{\mathcal{M}}(B_{1},B_{2}):={\mathcal{K}}(A_{1},A_{2})
, for some fixed choice of liftings
(A_{1},\zeta_{1})
,
(A_{2},\zeta_{2})
for any
B_{1},B_{2}\in{\mathcal{B}}
, is a
{\mathcal{B}}
bimodule.
Isomorphisms
\mu
and
\nu
yield an isomorphism

{\mathcal{K}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A^{\prime}_{2})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{M}}(q(A^{\prime}_{1}),q(A^{\prime}_{2}))
for any pair
(A^{\prime}_{1},A^{\prime}_{2})
of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Let
(Q,T)
be a pair of objects in a
k
-linear,
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
-finite exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
.
The universal extension
of
Q
by
T
is a unique up to isomorphism object
R
that fits into a conflation:

(12)

\displaystyle T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(Q,T)^{\vee}\to R\xrightarrow{\pi}Q

given by the canonical element in
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)^{\vee})
corresponding to
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)}
under the isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)^{\vee})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)^{\vee}
.

The
universal coextension
is a unique up to isomorphism object
U
that fits into a conflation

T\to U\to Q\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)

given by the canonical element in
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T),T)
corresponding to
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)}
under the isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T),T)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)^{\vee}
.

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a perpendicular torsion pair in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
with functors
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
,
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
,
\iota_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
,
\iota^{!}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
as in Proposition
2.3
.

We define the
{\mathcal{T}}
-projective subcategory
as a full subcategory of
{\mathcal{E}}
with objects

(13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}=\{E\in{\mathcal{E}}\,|\,\forall T\in{\mathcal{T}}\,\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(E,T)=0,\,(-)\circ\eta_{E}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E,T)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E,T)\},

where
\eta_{E}\colon E\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
is the adjunction unit.

We assume that
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq k\textrm{-vect}
and
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
is an indecomposable object. Then
the category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-projective objects is the full subcategory of
{\mathcal{E}}
whose objects are universal extensions of objects in
{\mathcal{F}}
:

Theorem 2.15
.

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a perpendicular torsion pair in a
k
-linear,
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
-finite exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq k\textrm{-vect}
. Let further
{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be the
{\mathcal{T}}
-projective subcategory. Then functor
q=\delta^{*}|_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
is a square-zero extension. Its kernel
{\mathcal{K}}(F_{1},F_{2})
is
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}F_{1},T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}F_{2},T)^{\vee}
with the natural
{\mathcal{F}}
bimodule structure (see Lemma
2.14
).

Proof.

First we check that
q
is essentially surjective. Let
F
be an object of
{\mathcal{F}}
and
Y_{F}
the universal extension of
F
by
T
. Then the conflation (
12
) with
Q=F
and
R=Y_{F}
is the decomposition of
Y_{F}
with respect to the torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
. Applying
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(-,T)
yields an exact sequence (see (
2
)):

\displaystyle 0\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(F,T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y_{F},T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(F,T)\xrightarrow{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(F,T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(Y_{F},T)\to 0.

It follows that
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(Y_{F},T)=0
and
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(F,T)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y_{F},T)
, hence
Y_{F}\in{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Conversely, let

(14)

T\otimes V\to Y\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y

be a decomposition of an object
Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
with respect to the torsion pair (see Proposition
2.3
). The conditions
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(Y,T)=0
and
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y,T)
imply that the map
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(T\otimes V,T)\simeq V^{\vee}\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)
obtained by applying
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(-,T)
to (
14
) is an isomorphism.
The element in
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T\otimes V)
corresponding to (
14
) is then the image of
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T\otimes V}
under the morphism
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T\otimes V,T\otimes V)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T\otimes V)
(which we proved to be an isomorphism). As
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T\otimes V}
corresponds to
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{V}
under the isomorphism

\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T\otimes V,T\otimes V)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(T,T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(V,V)\simeq k\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(V,V)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(V,V),

(
14
) corresponds to the identity on
V\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}
, i.e. to the canonical element in
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}
.
This implies that
Y
is isomorphic to the universal extension of
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y
by
T
.

Let
Y,Y^{\prime}
be objects of
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\varphi\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
a morphism.
As discussed above
\iota^{!}(Y)\simeq T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}
, and analogously for
Y^{\prime}
. Let
\alpha\colon\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T)^{\vee}\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}
be the transpose to
(-)\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\varphi\colon\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T)
, i.e.
\alpha(\zeta(-))=\zeta(-\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\varphi)
.
Then diagram

\textstyle{T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y}
\textstyle{T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T}\otimes\alpha}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}\delta^{*}\varphi}

commutes, i.e.
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T}\otimes\alpha=\iota^{!}(\varphi)
.

If

\delta^{*}\varphi
is an isomorphism, then so is
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T}\otimes\alpha
. Then the five lemma
[
Büh10
, Corollary 3.2]
implies that
\varphi
is an isomorphism, i.e. functor
q
is conservative.

Since
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},T)=0
, applying
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},-)
to (
14
) yields a short exact sequence

(15)

0\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}F,T)^{\vee}\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},T)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},Y)\xrightarrow{\Phi}\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y)\to 0.

The composition of
\Phi
with the adjunction isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},\delta^{*}Y)
is the map induced by
\delta^{*}
, hence
q
is full.

It follows from (
15
) that the kernel of
q
is

(16)

{\mathcal{K}}(Y^{\prime},Y)=\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(Y^{\prime},T)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T),

where the isomorphism follows from the fact that
Y^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Note that, the right hand side of (
16
) has the
{\mathcal{Y}}
bimodule structure; the right
{\mathcal{Y}}
module structure comes from the isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee})
, hence it is given by
(\zeta\otimes f)g=\zeta\otimes f\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}g
. The left
{\mathcal{Y}}
module structure, induced by the right
{\mathcal{Y}}
module structure of
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}
, reads
h(\zeta\otimes f)=\zeta(-\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}h)\otimes f
.

We check that (
16
) is an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{Y}}
- bimodules. Let
\zeta\otimes f\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime},T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}^{1}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee})
be a morphism in
{\mathcal{K}}(Y^{\prime},Y)
and let
g\colon Y_{0}\to Y^{\prime}

be a morphism in
{\mathcal{Y}}
. The composite
Y_{0}\xrightarrow{g}Y^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\eta_{Y^{\prime}}}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\zeta\otimes f}T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}
factors via
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{0}
as
Y_{0}
is an object in
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Hence,

(\zeta\otimes f)\circ\eta_{Y^{\prime}}\circ g=(\zeta\otimes f)\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}g\circ\eta_{Y_{0}}=(\zeta\otimes f\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}g)\circ\eta_{Y_{0}}
. It follows that
\zeta\otimes f\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}g
is the corresponding element in
{\mathcal{K}}(Y_{0},Y)
.

Let now
h\colon Y\to Y_{1}
be a morphism in
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\zeta\otimes f
as above. It follows from (
15
) that
\zeta\otimes f\in{\mathcal{K}}(Y^{\prime},Y)
should be considered as morphism
Y^{\prime}\to T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y,T)^{\vee}
. Then, the left
{\mathcal{Y}}
module structure is given by the composition with
\iota^{!}(h)
. Hence, by the above discussion on
\iota^{!}\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
, we have
h\circ(\zeta\otimes f)=\zeta(-\circ\delta_{*}\delta^{*}h)\otimes f
.

Let
\varphi\colon Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
be in
{\mathcal{K}}(Y_{1},Y_{2})
and
\psi\colon Y_{2}\to Y_{3}
in
{\mathcal{K}}(Y_{2},Y_{3})
. As
Y_{2}
is an object in
{\mathcal{Y}}
, any morphism
Y_{2}\to T
factors via
Y_{2}\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{2}
.Hence, the composite
\psi\circ\varphi
is

Y_{1}\to T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{2},T)^{\vee}\to Y_{2}\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{2}\to T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{3},T)^{\vee}\to Y_{3}.

As
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a torsion pair, there are no non-zero maps
T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{2},T)^{\vee}\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}Y_{2}
, hence
\psi\circ\varphi=0
which proves that
q
is a square-zero extension.

By Lemma
2.14
the kernel
{\mathcal{K}}
of
q
is an
{\mathcal{F}}
bimodule. For
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
the universal extension
Y_{F}
of
F
by
T
is a lifting of
F
to
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Any lift of a morphism
f\colon F\to F^{\prime}
to a morphism
g\colon Y_{F}\to Y_{F^{\prime}}
of universal extensions fits into a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}F^{\prime},T)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{F^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}F^{\prime}}
\textstyle{T\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}F,T)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T}\otimes\alpha}
\textstyle{Y_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}

with
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{T}\otimes\alpha=\iota^{!}(g)
induced by the composition with
f
(see above).
It follows from (
16
) that

{\mathcal{K}}(F_{1},F_{2})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\delta_{*}F_{1},T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(\delta_{*}F_{2},T)^{\vee}.

As (
16
) is an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{Y}}
bimodules, the above is an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{F}}
bimodules for the natural
{\mathcal{F}}
bimodule structure of the right hand side.
∎

2.6.
Strict admissible filtrations in exact categories

We define the
right admissible poset

\textrm{rAdm}({\mathcal{E}})
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
as the poset of right admissible subcategories with the inclusion order. Similarly, we consider the
left admissible poset

\textrm{lAdm}({\mathcal{E}})
. Note that two elements in
\textrm{rAdm}({\mathcal{E}})
and in
\textrm{lAdm}({\mathcal{E}})
in general have neither union nor intersection.

Consider a finite lattice
{\mathcal{L}}
with the maximal element
1
and the minimal element
0
.

Definition 2.16
.

A
right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{rAdm}({\mathcal{E}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
, such that

(Ri)

{\mathcal{T}}_{0}=0
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}={\mathcal{E}}
,

(Rii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{T}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}^{\perp}={\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{J}^{\perp}
.

Definition 2.17
.

A
left admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{lAdm}({\mathcal{E}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
, such that

(Li)

{\mathcal{F}}_{0}=0
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}={\mathcal{E}}
,

(Lii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{J}
,
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J}={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{J}
.

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
be a pair of right admissible subcategories in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
{\mathcal{S}}
is right admissible in
{\mathcal{T}}
. Similarly for left admissible subcategories.

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{S}}
is right admissible in
{\mathcal{E}}
, for any object
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
, there exists a conflation
S\to T\to T^{\prime}
with
S\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
T^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\perp}
, where the perpendicular is taken in
{\mathcal{E}}
. Since
T^{\prime}
is the cokernel of an inflation in
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}
is right admissible,
T^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
.
∎

The following definition for exact categories is a counterpart of the similar definition for triangulated categories in
[
BB18
]
(see also subsection
2.8
).

Definition 2.19
.

A
right admissible

{\mathcal{L}}
-
filtration
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is
strict
if

(Riii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
, we have
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}={\mathcal{T}}_{J}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}
as full subcategories of
{\mathcal{E}}
.

A
left admissible

{\mathcal{L}}
-
filtration
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is
strict
if

(Liii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
, we have
{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J}\cap(^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{J})={\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap(^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
as full subcategories of
{\mathcal{E}}
.

The conditions of strictness are trivially satisfied when
I\preceq J
or
J\preceq I
, hence they always hold for a full order
{\mathcal{L}}
.

For a lattice
{\mathcal{L}}
, denote by
{\mathcal{L}}^{op}
the lattice obtained by inversion of the order of elements in
{\mathcal{L}}
. For
I\in{\mathcal{L}}
, denote by
I^{o}
the corresponding element of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
. We have:
(I\cap J)^{o}=I^{o}\cup J^{o}
and
(I\cup J)^{o}=I^{o}\cap J^{o}
.

For a right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
\{{\mathcal{T}}_{I}\}
define the
right dual

{\mathcal{L}}^{op}
-
filtration

\{{\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}}\}
to consist of perpendicular categories:

{\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}}:={\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}

In view of Proposition
2.6
, this is an involutive duality between right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtrations and left admissible
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
-filtrations in an exact category.

Lemma 2.20
.

A right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is strict if and only if its right dual
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
-filtration is strict.

Proof.

This follows from equalities:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}\cup J^{o}}\cap(^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{J^{o}})={\mathcal{F}}_{(I\cap J)^{o}}\cap(^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{J^{o}})={\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{J}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}}\cap(^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}\cap J^{o}})={\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}}\cap({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{(I\cup J)^{o}})={\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}.

∎

Proposition 2.21
.

A right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
\{{\mathcal{T}}_{I}\}
on an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is strict if and only if, for any pair
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
, the category
{\mathcal{X}}={\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}
is a direct sum of mutually perpendicular subcategories
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}=({\mathcal{T}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp})
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}=({\mathcal{T}}_{J}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp})
.

Proof.

Assume that the
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration is strict. Lemma
2.18
implies existence of perpendicular torsion pairs
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{X}}_{I})
,
({\mathcal{T}}_{J},{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
,
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}
,
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
, and
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{I})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
.
As
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
, and
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{J}

categories
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
are mutually perpendicular, i.e.
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(X_{I},X_{J})
and
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(X_{J},X_{I})
vanish for any
X_{I}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
and
X_{J}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
.

Consider
X\in{\mathcal{X}}
. Decomposition (
4
) with respect to torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{X}}_{I})
is a conflation

(17)

T_{I}\to X\to X_{I}

with
T_{I}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
and
X_{I}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
. Denote by
\iota^{!}_{K}
the right adjoint to the inclusion functor
{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\ \to{\mathcal{E}}
, for any
K\in{\mathcal{L}}
. Functor
\iota_{I\cap J}^{!}
vanishes on
X
in view of torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}})
. Moreover,

\iota_{I\cap J}^{!}(X)=\iota_{I\cap J}^{!}\circ\iota_{I}^{!}(X)=\iota_{I\cap J}^{!}(T_{I})=0
. Therefore, in view of the torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
, we have:
T_{I}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
. Then perpendicularity of
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
implies that conflation (
17
) splits, hence
{\mathcal{X}}
is a direct sum of
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
.

Conversely, assume that
{\mathcal{X}}={\mathcal{X}}_{I}\oplus{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
, and
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
are mutually perpendicular. We need to show that
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}={\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}\cap{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}_{I}
.
By Lemma
2.18
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}
is admissible in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
. Then in view of definition of
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
, category
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
admits a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
. Since both components of the torsion pair are (left) perpendicular to
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
, we have:
{\mathcal{X}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}
. The other way around, any
X\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}\cap{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}_{I}
is in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}
, hence
X\in{\mathcal{X}}={\mathcal{X}}_{I}\oplus{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
. As
X
is right perpendicular to
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
its
{\mathcal{X}}_{J}
-component should be trivial, hence
X\in{\mathcal{X}}_{I}
.
∎

2.7.
Weakly idempotent split categories and torsion pairs

Recall
[
TT90
]
,
[
Büh10
]
that a category
{\mathcal{E}}
is
weakly idempotent split
if any retraction
r\colon E\to E^{\prime}
, i.e. a morphism admitting
s\colon E^{\prime}\to E
such that
rs=\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}
has a kernel. Equivalently, any co-retraction
c\colon E\to E^{\prime\prime}
, i.e. a morphism admitting
s\colon E^{\prime\prime}\to E
with
sc=\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{E}
, has a cokernel. Note that
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly idempotent split if and only if
{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is.

Lemma 2.22
.

[
Büh10
, Corollary 7.7]

An exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly idempotent split if and only if it has the following property: if
f\colon A\to B
is a deflation and
g\colon B\to C
a morphism such that
gf\colon A\to B
is a delfation, then
g
is a deflation.

We will show that an exact category possessing a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is weakly idempotent split if and only if
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
are.

Lemma 2.23
.

Consider a diagram in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
with conflations as rows:

(18)

\textstyle{C^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{B^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime\prime}}

If
g^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime\prime}
are deflations, so is
g
.

Proof.

Consider
{\mathcal{E}}
as a fully exact subcategory in an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
. The snake lemma for the diagram (
18
) considered as a diagram in
{\mathcal{A}}
yields a short exact sequence
0\to A^{\prime}\to A\to A^{\prime\prime}\to 0
of the kernels of the vertical maps. Since
g^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime\prime}
are deflations,
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
. As
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is closed under extensions,
A\in{\mathcal{E}}
. The snake lemma implies that cokernel of
g
in
{\mathcal{A}}
is trivial. We conclude that
A\to B\xrightarrow{g}C
is a conflation in
{\mathcal{E}}
, as the embedding
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
reflects exactness.
∎

Proposition 2.24
.

Consider an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
with a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
. Then
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly idempotent split if and only if
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
are.
