Proof.

Subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}
is closed under cokernels and
{\mathcal{F}}
is closed under kernels. Hence, if
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly idempotent split, then so are
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Now let
{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
be weakly idempotent split. Let
g\colon B\to C
be a morphism and
f\colon A\to B
a deflation such that the composite
gf
is a deflation. As adjoint-to-embedding functors
\iota^{!}:{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
and
\delta^{*}:{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
are exact, morphisms
\iota^{!}(f)
,
\iota^{!}(gf)
,
\delta^{*}(f)
,
\delta^{*}(gf)
are deflations. Hence, by Lemma
2.22
,
\iota^{!}g
and
\delta^{*}g
are deflations. Then
g
is a deflation by Lemma
2.23
. We conclude by Lemma
2.22
that
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly idempotent split.
∎

2.8.
Deriving admissibility

Given an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
, its bounded (resp. bounded above, bounded below, unbounded) derived category
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
, (resp.
{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{D}}^{+}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{E}})
) is the Verdier quotient of the homotopy category
{\mathcal{K}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
(resp.
{\mathcal{K}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{K}}^{+}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{K}}({\mathcal{E}})
) of bounded (bounded above, bounded below, unbounded) complexes in
{\mathcal{E}}
by the thick subcategory
{\mathcal{A}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
, (resp.
{\mathcal{A}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{+}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{E}})
) generated by acyclic complexes
[
TT90
]
,
[
Nee90
]
. If
{\mathcal{E}}
is a weakly idempotent split exact category, then
{\mathcal{A}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{+}({\mathcal{E}})
are already thick subcategories ((partial) boundedness of complexes is important here,
{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{E}})
is thick if
{\mathcal{E}}
is idempotent split, see
[
Nee90
]
).
The derived categories of exact categories are triangulated.

A full triangulated subcategory
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{D}}
in a triangulated category
{\mathcal{D}}
is said to be
right admissible

[
Bon89
]
if the inclusion functor
\iota_{0*}\colon{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{D}}
admits a right adjoint
\iota_{0}^{!}\colon{\mathcal{D}}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
. Equivalently,
{\mathcal{D}}
admits a semi-orthogonal decomposition (or SOD)
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\perp_{0}},{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\rangle
, see
[
Bon89
, Lemma 3.1]
. Similarly,
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{D}}
is
left admissible
if the inclusion functor admits a left adjoint
i_{0}^{*}\colon{\mathcal{D}}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
. Then
{\mathcal{D}}
admits an SOD
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle{\mathcal{D}}_{0},{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\rangle
.

Theorem 2.25
.

Let
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
be a left admissible subcategory of a weakly idempotent split exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}})
is a left admissible subcategory of
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
admits a semi-orthogonal decomposition
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})=\langle{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}})\rangle
.

Proof.

Since
\delta_{*}
is exact, its derived functor
R\delta_{*}
exists.

[
Kel96
, Theorem 12.1.b]
states that
R\delta_{*}
is fully faithful if for any conflation
\delta_{*}F^{\prime}\to E\to E^{\prime\prime}
in
{\mathcal{E}}
there exists a conflation
F^{\prime}\to F\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
{\mathcal{F}}
and a commutative diagram with identical the left-most vertical morphism:

\textstyle{\delta_{*}F^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}F^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\delta_{*}F^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

By Lemma
2.9
the conflation
F^{\prime}\to\delta^{*}E\to\delta^{*}E^{\prime\prime}
fits, hence
R\delta_{*}
is fully faithful.

Functor
\delta^{*}
is exact by Theorem
2.11
, hence its derived functor
L\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}})
exists and is left adjoint to
R\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
(
cf.

[
Kel96
, Lemma 13.6]
). Therefore,
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}})
is a left admissible in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Category
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}})
is the kernel of
L\delta^{*}
. It remains to show that if
E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
is a complex in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
such that
L\delta^{*}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
is acyclic then
E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a complex in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}})
.

Let
E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
be any complex in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
. The decomposition (
4
) with respect to the torsion pair
({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}})
applied term-wise to
E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
yields a sequence
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\xrightarrow{\eta^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
of complexes over
{\mathcal{E}}
. As
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E^{n}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{n}}E^{n}\xrightarrow{\eta^{n}}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{n}
is a conflation for every
n
,
[
Kel96
, Lemma 11.6]
implies that
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\to E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\to\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}[1]
is an exact triangle in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
.
Since functors
\delta_{*}
and
\delta^{*}
are exact,
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\simeq R\delta_{*}L\delta^{*}(E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}})
. If
E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
lies in the kernel of
L\delta^{*}
complex
\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
is acyclic. Therefore,
\varepsilon^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}
is a quasi-isomorphism, i.e.
E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\simeq\iota_{*}\iota^{*}E^{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}})
.
∎

Next we will show that a strict right/left admissible filtration on an exact category induces a strict right/left admissible filtration on its derived category as a triangulated category.

Recall the definitions after
[
BB18
]
.
A triangulated subcategory
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
in a triangulated category
{\mathcal{D}}
is
admissible
if it is both right and left admissible, if and only if
{\mathcal{D}}
admits SODs:
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\perp_{0}},{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\rangle=\langle{\mathcal{D}}_{0},{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\rangle
. This condition implies existence of a recollement
[
BBD82
]
:

(19)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}={\mathcal{D}}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
,
j^{*}
is the quotient functor,
j_{!}
embeds
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
as
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
while
j_{*}
embeds
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
as
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\perp_{0}}
. Conversly, for any recollement (
19
), category
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
is admissible. We say that (
19
) is a
recollement with respect to subcategory

{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
.

Given
t
-struc
tures
({\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\leq 0},{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\geq 1})
on
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
and
({\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{\leq 0},{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{\geq 1})
on
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
, there exists a unique
t
-struc
ture
({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq 0},{\mathcal{D}}^{\geq 1})
on
{\mathcal{D}}
such that functors
i_{0*}
and
j^{*}
are
t
-exact, i.e.
i_{*}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\leq 0}\subset{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq 0}
,
i_{*}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}^{\geq 1}\subset{\mathcal{D}}^{\geq 1}
,
j^{*}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq 0}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{\leq 0}
, and
j^{*}{\mathcal{D}}^{\geq 1}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{\geq 1}
, see
[
BBD82
]
. The
t
-struc
ture
({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq 0},{\mathcal{D}}^{\geq 1})
is said to be
glued via the recollement (
19
)
.

We define the
right admissible poset

\textrm{rAdm}({\mathcal{D}})
of a triangulated category
{\mathcal{D}}
as the poset of right admissible subcategories with the inclusion order. Similarly, we consider the
left admissible poset

\textrm{lAdm}({\mathcal{D}})
and the
admissible poset

\textrm{Adm}({\mathcal{D}})
.

For a finite lattice
{\mathcal{L}}
with the maximal element
1
and the minimal element
0
a
right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on
{\mathcal{D}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{rAdm}({\mathcal{D}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{D}}_{I}
, such that

(Rti)

{\mathcal{D}}_{0}=0
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}={\mathcal{D}}
,

(Rtii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{D}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{J}
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cup J}^{\perp_{0}}={\mathcal{D}}_{I}^{\perp_{0}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{J}^{\perp_{0}}
.

A
left admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on
{\mathcal{D}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{lAdm}({\mathcal{D}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{D}}_{I}
, such that

(Lti)

{\mathcal{D}}_{0}=0
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}={\mathcal{D}}
,

(Ltii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{D}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{J}
,
{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cup J}={}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}_{J}
.

An
admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on
{\mathcal{D}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{Adm}({\mathcal{D}})
which defines both left and right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{D}}
.

Denote by
{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
the Verdier quotient. We say that a (right or left) admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{D}}
is
strict
if

(iii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cup J}/{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cap J}}({\mathcal{D}}_{I}/{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{D}}_{J}/{\mathcal{D}}_{I\cap J})=0
.

Theorem 2.26
.

Consider a finite lattice
{\mathcal{L}}
and a strict right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
\{{\mathcal{T}}_{I}\}
on a weakly idempotent split exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
\{{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})\}
is a strict right admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

Consider
I\in{\mathcal{L}}
. Theorem
2.25
implies that
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
is right admissible.
Since condition (Rti) is obviously satisfied, it suffices to check conditions (Rtii) and (iii). We note that
condition (iii) follows immediately from the direct sum decomposition
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}=({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I})\oplus({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{J})
of Proposition
2.21
.

Let
I,J
be any pair of elements of
{\mathcal{L}}
. Lemma
2.18
and Proposition
2.21
imply the existence of the perpendicular torsion pairs
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{X}}_{I})
,
({\mathcal{T}}_{J},{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
,
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{I}\oplus{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}
,
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{J})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
, and
({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J},{\mathcal{X}}_{I})
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
.

Clearly
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J})\subset{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{J})
. Let now
X
be an object of
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{J})
. The decompositions of
X
with respect to SOD’s
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})=\langle{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{X}}_{J}),{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J})\rangle
,
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{J})=\langle{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{X}}_{I}),{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J})\rangle
(given by Theorem
2.25
) yield isomorphic exact triangles
T_{IJ}\to X\to Y
,
T^{\prime}_{IJ}\to X\to Y^{\prime}
, because morphisms
T_{IJ}\to X
,
T^{\prime}_{IJ}\to X
are the adjunction counit for the embedding
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{J})
. Then

Y\simeq Y^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{X}}_{I})\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{X}}_{J})
=0.
Hence
X\simeq T_{IJ}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J})
, which proves the first part of (Rtii).

By Lemma
2.20
the right dual
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{E}}
is strict. Then the same argument for the opposite category
{\mathcal{E}}^{op}
implies that
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}})\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}_{J^{o}})={\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}_{I^{o}\cap J^{o}})
, where
{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{o}}:={\mathcal{T}}_{K}^{\perp}
.
By Theorem
2.25
,
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}_{K^{o}})={}^{\perp_{0}}{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{T}}_{K})
and
{I^{o}\cap J^{o}}=(I\cup J)^{o}
, hence the second part of (Rtii) follows.
∎

3.
Thin categories

In this section we introduce the basic notion for our approach to highest weight categories, thin exact categories. We define a canonical partial order on the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects and construct a strict admissible filtration related to the poset. Then we find projective generators in thin categories. This is based on the construction of ’universal extensions’ of standarizable collections by Dlab and Ringel
[
DR92
]
.

3.1.
Canonical strict filtrations on thin categories

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field and
k\rm{-vect}
the category of finite dimensional
k
-vector spaces.

Definition 3.1
.

We say that a
k
-linear Hom and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
-finite
exact category

{\mathcal{E}}
is
thin
if it has a right admissible filtration
0={\mathcal{T}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\subset\ldots\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{n}={\mathcal{E}}
with graded factors
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}:={\mathcal{T}}_{i-1}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
equivalent to

k\rm{-vect}
, for all
i\in[1,n]
.

Any filtration with above properties in a thin category is called
thin filtration
.

The notion of thin filtration, hence of thin category, is self-dual: if we transfer to the opposite category, then
{\mathcal{T}}_{i}^{\perp}
make a right admissible filtration with graded factors
k\rm{-vect}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

A thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
is weakly idempotent split.

Proof.

Since
k\rm{-vect}
is idempotent split, the statement follows from Proposition
2.24
.
∎

Object
E
in an exact category is called
irreducible
if any inflation
A\to E
is either zero or an isomorphism.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a thin category and
E_{i}\in{\mathcal{E}}
the object corresponding to

k
under the equivalence
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\simeq k\textrm{-vect}
for a thin filtration. Then
\Lambda=\{E_{1},\ldots,E_{n}\}
is the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

This follows easily by induction on the length of a thin filtration in view of decompositions (
4
) for right admissible subcategories in the filtration.
∎

Recall
[
Bon89
]
that an ordered collection
(E_{n},\ldots,E_{1})
is a
full exceptional sequence
in a
k
-linear triangulated category
{\mathcal{T}}
if

•

\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{T}}}(E_{i},E_{i}[l])
equals
k
if
l=0
and vanishes otherwise,

•

\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{T}}}(E_{i},E_{j}[l])
vanish for
i
less than
j
and any
l\in\mathbb{Z}
,

•

{\mathcal{T}}
is equivalent to its smallest triangulated subcategory containing
E_{n},\ldots,E_{1}
.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
\{E_{1},\ldots,E_{n}\}
be the ordered collection of representatives of isomorphisms classes of irreducible objects in a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
(E_{n},\ldots,E_{1})
is a full exceptional sequence in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

Follows from Theorem
2.26
.
∎

We endow the set
\Lambda
of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in a thin category with a
canonical poset structure
. Namely, we consider the minimal partial order such that

(20)

{\rm if}\ E_{i}\notin E_{j}^{\perp},\ {\rm for\ some}\ i,j\in\Lambda,{\rm then}\ i\preceq j.

The existence of a thin filtration implies no cycle

(i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{k}=i_{1})
, such that
E_{i_{s}}\notin E_{i_{t}}^{\perp}
for
i_{s}\leq i_{t}
.
Therefore, the partial order is well-defined. The obvious full order on
\Lambda
induced by any thin filtration is a linearization of the canonical partial order.
We write
E(\lambda)
for the irreducible object of
{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

If
{\mathcal{L}}
is a finite distributive lattice then by Birkhoff’s theorem
[
Bir37
]
,
{\mathcal{L}}
is isomorphic to the lattice of lower ideals
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
in a finite poset
\Lambda
. A
lower (upper) ideal
in a poset
\Lambda
is a subset
I\subset\Lambda
(resp.
U\subset\Lambda
) which together with every
\lambda\in I
(resp.
\lambda\in U
) contains all
\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
\lambda^{\prime}\preceq\lambda
(resp.
\lambda^{\prime}\succeq\lambda
).
The poset
\Lambda
in Birkhoff’s theorem is identified with the subposet of join-prime elements in
{\mathcal{L}}
. An element
s\in{\mathcal{L}}
is
join-prime
if the fact that
s\preceq J_{1}\cup J_{2}
, for some
J_{1},J_{2}\in{\mathcal{L}}
, implies that
s\preceq J_{1}
or
s\preceq J_{2}
.

For a finite poset
\Lambda
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
, the corresponding join-prime element in the distributive lattice
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)

is the principal lower ideal
I_{\lambda}\subset\Lambda
, i.e. the ideal of all elements
\mu\in\Lambda
, such that
\mu\preceq\lambda
. Then
I_{<\lambda}:=I_{\lambda}\setminus\{\lambda\}
is an ideal in
I_{\lambda}
which contains all ideals
J\subsetneqq I_{\lambda}
.

Let
\{{\mathcal{T}}_{I}\}
be a right admissible
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
-filtration. To
\lambda\in\Lambda
we assign the subcategory

{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}:={\mathcal{T}}_{I_{<\lambda}}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{\lambda}},

the
\lambda
-graded factor of the filtration. By Lemma
2.18
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}
is left admissible in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{\lambda}}
. Similarly, given a left admissible
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
-filtration
\{{\mathcal{F}}_{I}\}
, we define the
\lambda
-graded factor by:

{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}:={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I_{<\lambda}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{I_{\lambda}}.

For a class of objects
\{X_{i}\}
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
, we denote by
{\mathcal{F}}(\{X_{i}\})
the smallest fully exact subcategory in
{\mathcal{E}}
that contains all
X_{i}
’s.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a thin category and
\Lambda
the canonical poset of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{E({\lambda})\}_{\lambda\in I})
, where
I
runs over the set of lower ideals in
\Lambda
,
defines a strict right admissible
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
-filtration on
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

Category
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\notin I}\})
belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}
and any thin filtration on
{\mathcal{E}}
gives a filtration on every object of
{\mathcal{E}}
with all graded factors sums of irreducible objects, i.e. either in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
or in
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
. By Lemma (
2.7
),
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
is right admissible and
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}={\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}
.

Subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
is contained in the left perpendicular to both
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{J}
, hence, by the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J}
, it is contained in the left perpendicular to
{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J}
, i.e. in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}
. Since the opposite inclusion is obvious,
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{T}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
and, similarly,
{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J}={\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{J}
, i.e.
I\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
defines a right admissible filtration.

Finally, as
{\mathcal{F}}(\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in J\setminus I})
is the right perpendicular category to
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
and to
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cup J}
, for any lower ideals
I\subset J
, the filtration is strict.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

In the case when the field
k
is not algebraically closed, we generalize the definition of a thin category as follows: we say that a
k
-linear Hom and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
-finite
exact category

{\mathcal{E}}
is
thin
if it has a right admissible filtration
0={\mathcal{T}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\subset\ldots\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{n}={\mathcal{E}}
such that, for all
i\in[1,n]
, the graded factor
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}:={\mathcal{T}}_{i-1}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
is equivalent to
\textrm{mod-}\Gamma_{i}
, for a finite dimensional division
k
-algebra
\Gamma_{i}
. The construction of the partially ordered set
\Lambda
and the
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
-filtration on
{\mathcal{E}}
are basically the same.

3.2.
Projective generators in thin categories

An object
P\in{\mathcal{E}}
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is
projective
if functor
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(P,-)\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}b
is exact. It is equivalent to requiring
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(P,-)=0
. A full additive subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is
projectively generating
if all objects of
{\mathcal{P}}
are projective and, for any object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
, there exists a deflation
P\to E
with
P\in{\mathcal{P}}
.

For a collection of objects
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
, denote by
\textrm{add }\{X_{i}\}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
the full subcategory of finite direct sums of copies of
X_{i}
’s.
A set of projective object
\{P_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is a
set of projective generators
if
\textrm{add }\{P_{i}\}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is projectively generating. If the set consists of one element
P
, we say that
P
is a
projective generator
. A
set of injective generators
is a set of objects in
{\mathcal{E}}
which is a set of projective generators in the opposite category
{\mathcal{E}}^{op}
.

The following proposition allows us to construct a projective generator in an exact category, given an admissible subcategory isomorphic to
k
-vect and a projective generator in its perpendicular.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq k-{\rm vect}
be a right admissible subcategory of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Let
Q
be a projective object in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
. Then the universal extension
R
of
Q
by the irreducible object
T
in
{\mathcal{T}}
, is projective in
{\mathcal{E}}
. If
\{Q_{i}\}
is a set of projective generators in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
, then
T\cup\{R_{i}\}
is a set of projective generators in
{\mathcal{E}}
, where
R_{i}
is the universal extension of
Q_{i}
by
T
.

Proof.

By applying the second of the sequences (
2
) to the conflation (
12
) and object
F\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
we get:
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(R,F)=0
.

Now applying (
2
) to the same conflation and object
T^{\oplus a}\in{\mathcal{T}}
gives an exact sequence:

(21)

\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T)\otimes\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(T,T^{\oplus a})\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(Q,T^{\oplus a})\to\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(R,T^{\oplus a})\to 0

Since the first morphism is an isomorphism,
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(R,T^{\oplus a})
vanishes.

Now any object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
has a conflation of the form

(22)

T^{\oplus a}\to E\to F

with
T^{\oplus a}\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
F\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
.
Applying
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(R,-)
to this conflation gives that
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(R,E)=0
, for any
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
, i.e.
R
is projective in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Applying
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(T,-)
to the same conflation implies that
T
is projective in
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Now let
\{Q_{i}\}
be a set of projective generators in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\perp}
. Consider conflation (
22
) for an arbitrary object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
. We have a deflation
\oplus Q_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\to F
. Since
R_{i}
is a universal extension of
Q_{i}
, we have a deflation
R_{i}\to Q_{i}
. As
\oplus R_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}
is a projective object in
{\mathcal{E}}
, the composite deflation
\oplus R_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\to\oplus Q_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\to F
lifts along the deflation
E\to F
to a morphism
\oplus R_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\to E
. We get a diagram:

\textstyle{T^{\oplus a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{T^{\oplus a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{T^{\oplus a}\oplus R_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus R_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

that satisfies the condition of Lemma
2.23
. Hence the morphism
T^{\oplus a}\oplus R_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\to E
is a deflation.
∎

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a thin category with the canonical poset
\Lambda
. There exist pairwise non-isomorphic, unique (up to a non-unique isomorphism) projective objects
\{P(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
with local endomorphism rings which admit deflations
d_{\lambda}\colon P(\lambda)\to E(\lambda)
. The kernel of
d_{\lambda}
is in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{<\lambda}}

and the subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{P(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda})
is projectively generating.

Proof.

We construct pairwise non-isomorphic
P(\lambda)
by induction on
|\Lambda|
. The case
|\Lambda|=1
is clear. Consider
\lambda\in\Lambda
. If
\lambda
is minimal then
P(\lambda)=E(\lambda)
. Otherwise, let
\nu\in\Lambda
be a minimal element. Category
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\neq\nu})
is thin with canonical poset
\Lambda\setminus\{\nu\}
, hence by inductive hypothesis, there exists
Q(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}
with the required properties. By Proposition
3.5
, category
{\mathcal{E}}
admits a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
where
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{F}}(E(\nu))
.

We define
P(\lambda)
as the universal extension of
Q(\lambda)
by
E(\nu)
. By Proposition
3.7
object
P(\lambda)
is projective in
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be the category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-projective objects. By Theorem
2.15
functor
\delta^{*}=q\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
is a square-zero extension.
As it was shown in the beginning of the proof of this theorem,
P(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{Y}}
and
q(P(\lambda))=Q(\lambda)
. Hence, the kernel of the surjective morphism
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(P(\lambda))\to\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(Q(\lambda))
induced by
q
is a square-zero ideal.
As
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(Q(\lambda))
is local, so is
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(P(\lambda))
.
By inductive hypothesis
Q(\lambda)
admits a deflation
Q(\lambda)\to E(\lambda)
with kernel in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{<\lambda}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then the composite of deflations
P(\lambda)\to Q(\lambda)\to E(\lambda)
is a deflation whose kernel lies in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{<\lambda}}
. This implies
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\lambda),E(\lambda))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(E(\lambda))
.

For
\lambda\notin I_{\mu}
,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\mu),E(\lambda))=0
, as
E(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{I_{\mu}}
,
P(\mu)\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{\mu}}
. Hence, if
P(\lambda)\simeq P(\mu)
, deflations
P(\lambda)\to E(\mu)
,
P(\mu)\to E(\lambda)
imply that
\mu\in I_{\lambda}
and
\lambda\in I_{\mu}
, i.e.
\lambda=\mu
.

Proposition
3.7
implies by induction that
{\mathcal{P}}
is a projectively generating subcategory.

Now we prove the uniqueness of
P(\lambda)
. Let
P(\lambda)
and
P^{\prime}(\lambda)
be two
projective objects
with local endomorphism rings
and
d_{\lambda}\colon P(\lambda)\to E(\lambda)
,
d^{\prime}_{\lambda}\colon P^{\prime}(\lambda)\to E(\lambda)
deflations. Morphism
d_{\lambda}
admits a lift to
\alpha\colon P(\lambda)\to P^{\prime}(\lambda)
. Similarly, we have
\beta\colon P^{\prime}(\lambda)\to P(\lambda)
. The composite
x=\beta\circ\alpha\in\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(P(\lambda))
is an element whose image under the surjective map
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(P(\lambda))\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\lambda),E(\lambda))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(E(\lambda))\simeq k
is the identity morphism.
As
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(P(\lambda))
is a local ring,

x
is invertible. Similarly,
\alpha\circ\beta
is invertible, which implies that
\alpha
is an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

Since the opposite of a thin category is thin, Proposition
3.8
implies that objects
E(\lambda)
admit also injective hulls
I(\lambda)
and
{\mathcal{I}}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{I(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is an injectively generating subcategory.

Remark 3.10
.

Theorem
2.15
remains true for a torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
with
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq\textrm{mod-}\Gamma
, for a division
k
-algebra
\Gamma
. The current proof works if the tensor product over
k
in the definition of the universal extension (
12
) and in Theorem
2.15

is replaced by the tensor product over
\Gamma
.
Hence, thin categories over arbitrary fields (see Remark
3.6
) admit projective covers
P(\lambda)
of
E(\lambda)
with local endomorphisms algebras.

4.
Abelian envelopes of exact categories

We shall introduce the left and right abelian envelopes of an exact category and discuss their basic properties. We construct such envelopes for exact categories with enough projectives and injectives. Under further finiteness conditions we prove the derived equivalence of an exact category with its envelopes.
Also, we discuss the structures which admissible subcategories and strict filtrations in exact categories induce on envelopes, and apply these techniques to thin categories.

For additive categories
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
with
{\mathcal{C}}
essentially small, we denote by
\mathop{\textrm{Fun}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{B}})
the category of additive functors
{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
with natural transformations as morphisms.

4.1.
Definition of the right and left envelope

There is a meaningful notion of righ/left exact functors between exact categories. We introduce a bit of their theory in the form required for our purposes.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
be exact categories. We define an additive functor
\Psi\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
to be
right exact
if for any conflation
X\xrightarrow{i}Y\xrightarrow{d}Z
in
{\mathcal{E}}
morphism
\Psi(d)
is a deflation with kernel
i^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to\Psi(Y)
and
\Psi(i)=i^{\prime}\circ d^{\prime}
, for some deflation
d^{\prime}\colon\Psi(X)\to X^{\prime}
. A
left exact
functor is defined similarly. We denote by
\textrm{Rex}({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
and
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
the categories of right/left exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
with natural transformations as morphisms.

Lemma 4.1
.

The composite of two right exact functors is right exact, and similarly for left exact functors.

Proof.

Let
\Psi\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
,
\Phi\colon{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime\prime}
be right exact functors and
X\xrightarrow{i}Y\xrightarrow{d}Z
a conflation in
{\mathcal{E}}
. Let
i^{\prime}
be the kernel of
\Psi(d)
and
\Psi(i)=i^{\prime}\circ d^{\prime}
. As
\Psi(d)
and
d^{\prime}
are deflations, so are
\Phi\Psi(d)
and
\Phi(d^{\prime})
. Let
i^{\prime\prime}
be the kernel of
\Phi\Psi(d)
. The right exactness of
\Phi
implies
\Phi(i^{\prime})=i^{\prime\prime}\circ d^{\prime\prime}
, for some deflation
d^{\prime\prime}
. Then
\Phi\Psi(i)=\Phi(i^{\prime})\circ\Phi(d^{\prime})=i^{\prime\prime}\circ d^{\prime\prime}\circ\Phi(d^{\prime})
is the composite of
i^{\prime\prime}
with the deflation
d^{\prime\prime}\circ\Phi(d^{\prime})
, i.e.
\Phi\Psi
is right exact.
∎

Note that, for an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
, an additive functor
\Psi\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is right exact if for any conflation (
1
) the sequence
\Psi(X)\to\Psi(Y)\to\Psi(Z)\to 0
is exact in
{\mathcal{A}}
.

The category
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
of contravariant left exact functors with values in abelian groups is the quotient of the category
\textrm{Fun}({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
of additive functors by the Serre subcategory
\textrm{Efc}({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
of effaceable functors (
cf.

[
Gab62
,
Kel90
]
), hence it is abelian. The composite

(23)

h\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to\textrm{Fun}({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)\to\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)

of the Yoneda embedding with the quotient functor
induces an equivalence of
{\mathcal{E}}
with a fully exact subcategory of
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
. Actually,

h
(
23
) is the embedding functor of the Gabriel-Quillen theorem that we mentioned in Section
2.1
.

Definition 4.2
.

A
right abelian envelope
for an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
together with a right exact functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
which, for any abelian category
{\mathcal{B}}
, yields an equivalence of categories
(-)\circ i_{R}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{B}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
.

If we choose a quasi-inverse to this equivalence then every right exact functor
F\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
has a unique, up to a canonical functorial isomorphism, lifting to a functor
\tilde{F}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{B}}
together with a canonical isomorphism
\tilde{F}\circ i_{R}\simeq F
:

(24)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{i_{R}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{F}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

Similarly, a
left abelian envelope
of
{\mathcal{E}}
is an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
together with a left exact functor
i_{L}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
which, for any abelian category
{\mathcal{B}}
, induces an equivalence
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{B}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
.

It easily follows from the definition that if a right (left) abelian envelope
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
(
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
) exists for a given exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
then it is unique up to a canonical equivalence, i.e. given two right abelian envelopes
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
, there is a unique, up to a unique isomorphism of functors, equivalence
\Phi\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

We say that
{\mathcal{E}}
is an exact category
with the right/left envelope
if
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
/
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
exists.

The opposite to an exact (abelian) category is again an exact (abelian) category. The opposite to the right exact functor is left exact. Then transfer to the opposite categories and functors in (
24
) implies that if
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
exists then so does
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
and

(25)

{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}.

The envelopes do not exist in general, see Example
4.7
below. We will discuss their existence under some conditions later in this section.
Historically, the first instance
when right and left abelian envelopes exist are
quasi-abelian categories.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an additive category with kernels and cokernels. A morphism
f\colon C\to C^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
is
strict
if the canonical map
\textrm{Coim }f\to\textrm{Im }f
is an isomorphism. Category
{\mathcal{C}}
is
quasi-abelian
if a pull back of a strict epimorphism is a strict epimorphism and a pushout of a strict monomorphism is a strict monomorphism. A quasi-abelian category
{\mathcal{C}}
carries an intrinsic exact category structure with strict monomorphisms as inflations and strict epimorphisms as deflations.

With
[
Sch99
, Proposition 1.2.33]
J-P. Schneiders constructed the right abelian envelope of a quasi-abelian category, which he denoted by
{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{H}}({\mathcal{C}})
. He proved that the corresponding functor
I\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{H}}({\mathcal{C}})
is fully faithful, exact, and reflects exactness
[
Sch99
, Corollary 1.2.27]
. Moreover,
I
induces an equivalence of unbounded derived categories
{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{H}}({\mathcal{C}}))

[
Sch99
, Proposition 1.2.31]
.

It make sense to compare the left/right envelopes with the exact abelian hull introduced by M. Adelaman.

In
[
Ade73
]
, M. Adelman defined and proved the existence of an
exact abelian hull
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
,
i.e. an abelian category
\mathcal{U}({\mathcal{E}})
together with an
exact functor
i_{e}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{U}({\mathcal{E}})
which induces an equivalence of the category of exact functors
\mathcal{U}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{B}}
with the category of exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
, for any abelian category
{\mathcal{B}}
,
cf.

[
Ste14
]
.
Moreover,
i_{e}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{e}({\mathcal{E}})
is fully faithful and reflects exactness, see
[
Ste14
, Theorem 4.4.4]
. It would be interesting to investigate the relation of the exact abelian hull of
{\mathcal{E}}
with its abelian envelopes.

The rest of this subsection was added after
the reviewer’s comments on possible relation of Rump’s recent construction of left/right quotient categories, which appeared at about the same time as the first version of our paper.

Recall that an additive category
{\mathcal{A}}
is
left abelian

[
Rum20
]
if it has cokernels and for any
f\colon A\to B
and
g\colon D\to B
in
{\mathcal{A}}
with
c=\textrm{cok }f
and
cg=0
, there is a cokernel
d\colon E\to D
and a morphism
e\colon E\to A
such that
gd=fe
:

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

In
[
Rum20
]
Rump introduced the left/right quotient categories, respectively
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
and
Q_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, for any left/right exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
.
For a left exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
, the category
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is defined as the quotient of the left abelian category
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{E}})
of finitely presented functors
{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\to\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits
by the Serre subcategory of
defects
, i.e. functors
F\colon{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\to\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits
which admit a presentation
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(-,Y)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(-,Z)\to F(-)\to 0
, with a deflation
Y\to Z
.
The category
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is itself left abelian. The Yoneda functor
{\mathcal{E}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{E}})
composed with the quotient functor gives a fully faithful exact functor
i\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
,
[
Rum20
, Proposition 3.5]
. Category
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})

has the following universal property for right exact functors from
{\mathcal{E}}
:

Proposition 4.3
.

[
Rum20
, Corollary 3.1]

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a left exact category. For any left abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
, the composition with
i
induces an equivalence of the category of additive functors
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{A}}
which respect cokernels of morphisms and right exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
.

Any exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is both left and right exact. The maximal exact structures on the quotient categories
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
,
Q_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
consist of all kernel-cokernel pairs. Rump introduced also a weaker exact structure on them, denoted by
Q_{l}^{\vee}({\mathcal{E}})
and
Q_{r}^{\vee}({\mathcal{E}})
, and proved that the exact abelian hull
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{E}})
of
{\mathcal{E}}
is equivalent to

{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq Q_{r}(Q_{l}^{\vee}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq Q_{l}(Q_{r}^{\vee}({\mathcal{E}})).

Theorem 4.4
.

If
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is abelian then
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
exists and is equivalent to
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

We need to check that if
Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is abelian it has the universal property of the right abelian envelope. It is well-known that a functor between abelian categories is right exact if and only if it respects cokernels (note that it is not so when categories are only left abelian).
Hence,
according to Proposition
4.3
, we have an equivalence
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits(Q_{l}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{A}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{A}})
, for any abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

4.2.
Faithfulness and fully faithfulness of the functor
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be an essentially small exact category with the right envelope
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
. Since the category
\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits
of abelian groups is abelian, the composition with
i_{R}
yields an equivalence

(26)

\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits).

The composite of the Yoneda embedding
h_{{\mathcal{A}}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
with the above equivalence is the fully faithful exact functor

(27)

\displaystyle\Xi\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits),

\displaystyle\Xi(A)(-)=\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}(i_{R}(-),A).

Recall after
[
KS06
, Definition 6.3.3]
that an object
C
in a category
{\mathcal{C}}
with small filtrant colimits is
compact
if for any
\alpha\colon I\to{\mathcal{C}}
with
I
small and filtrant the natural morphism
\varinjlim\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(C,\alpha)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(C,\varinjlim\alpha)
is an isomorphism. We denote by
{\mathcal{C}}^{c}
the full subcategory of compact objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be an essentially small exact category with the right abelian envelope. Then functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is faithful. Moreover,
\Xi
identifies
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
with the subcategory
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},{\mathcal{A}}b)^{c}
of compact objects in
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},{\mathcal{A}}b)
.

Proof.

Functor
h
in (
23
)
is exact, in particular right exact, hence there exists right exact functor
q\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},{\mathcal{A}}b)
such that
q\circ i_{R}=h
. As
h
is fully faithful, functor
i_{R}
is faithful.

Functor
\Xi\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
in (
27
) is exact and fully faithful. As category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is abelian,
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
is equivalent to the category
\textrm{Ind}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
of ind-objects over
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, see
[
KS06
, Corollary 8.6.3]
. The category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is idempotent complete, hence it is equivalent to the category of compact objects in
\textrm{Ind}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
, see
[
KS06
, Exercise 6.1]
. ∎

Remark 4.6
.

By Lemma
4.5
, if
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
exists then it is equivalent to the full subcategory of compact objects in
\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
. However, it is not clear if the composite of the universal functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
with the canonical embedding
\Xi\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)^{c}\to\mathop{\textrm{Lex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits)
is the Yoneda embedding
h
(
23
).

The following example of an exact category without the right abelian envelope was communicated to us by A. Efimov.

Example 4.7
.

Let
R
be a non-coherent ring and
{\mathcal{E}}
the category of finitely presented projective right
R
-modules. Then
{\mathcal{E}}
does not have the right abelian envelope. Indeed, by Lemma
4.5
, if
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
exists it is equivalent to the category of compact objects in
\textrm{Mod-}R
, i.e. to the category of finitely presented right
R
- modules (see
[
KS06
, Exercise 6.8]
) which, by the assumption on
R
, is not abelian.

By Lemma
4.5
, the functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is always faithful. It would be interesting to know whether functor it is also always full.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be an essentially small exact category. If the right abelian envelope
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
exists and
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is full then
i_{R}

induces an equivalence of
{\mathcal{E}}
with a fully exact subcategory of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

Since
i_{R}
is full, it is fully faithful by Lemma
4.5
. Then formula (
27
) implies that the composite of
\Xi
and
i_{R}
is the Yoneda embedding
h
(
23
):
\Xi\circ i_{R}\simeq h
.
As
i_{R}
is right exact,
\Xi
and
h
are exact, and
\Xi
is fully faithful, functor
i_{R}
is exact. Moreover, since by Gabriel-Quillen theorem
h

is an inclusion of a fully exact subcategory and
\Xi
is exact and fully faithful,
i_{R}
induces an equivalence of
{\mathcal{E}}
with a fully exact subcategory of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.
∎

4.3.
The monad associated to the right abelian envelope

We consider the 2-category
\mathscr{E}x_{r}
whose objects are exact categories admitting right abelian envelopes. The 1-morphisms in
\mathscr{E}x_{r}
are right exact functors and 2-morphisms are natural transformations.

Proposition 4.9
.

The 2-category
\mathscr{E}x_{r}
admits a pseudo-endofunctor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}\colon\mathscr{E}x_{r}\to\mathscr{E}x_{r}
and a pseudo-natural transformation
\mathscr{I}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{E}x_{r}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
such that, for any
{\mathcal{E}}\in\mathscr{E}x_{r}
, the functor
\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

We shall define the functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
and the natural transformation
\mathscr{I}
. For
{\mathcal{E}}\in\mathscr{E}x_{r}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}:=i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Let
\Phi\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{E}x_{r}}({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
. By Lemma
4.1
, the composite
\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\Phi\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
is right exact, hence there exists
\Psi\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{E}x_{r}}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}))
, unique up to a canonical functorial isomorphism, such that
\Psi\circ\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}}\simeq\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\Phi
. We put
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi):=\Psi
. Clearly,
\mathscr{I}
is a pseudo-natural transformation.

A 2-morphism in
\mathscr{E}x_{r}
, i.e. a natural transformation
\tau\colon\Phi_{1}\to\Phi_{2}
of right exact functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
, yields a natural transformation
\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\tau\colon\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\Phi_{1}\to\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\Phi_{2}
of functors in
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}))
. Equivalence
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}))
implies existence of a unique
\sigma\colon\Psi_{1}\to\Psi_{2}
, where
\Psi_{i}\circ\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}}\simeq\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\Phi_{i}
, for
i=1,2
:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}}

We put
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\tau):=\sigma
.

The uniqueness of
\Psi\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
such that
\Psi\circ\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}}\simeq\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}\circ\Phi
implies canonical isomorphisms
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi^{\prime}\circ\Phi)\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi^{\prime})\circ{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi)
, for
\Phi\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{E}x_{r}}({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
,
\Phi^{\prime}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{E}x_{r}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime\prime})
. It follows that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
is indeed a pseudo-functor.
∎

Recall that a
monad
on a category
{\mathcal{C}}
is an endofunctor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
together with natural transformations
\eta\colon\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}\to F
,
\mu\colon F^{2}\to F
which satisfy the standard conditions.

An
algebra over monad

(F,\eta,\mu)
is a pair
(C,T_{C})
of an object
C\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
T_{C}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(F(C),C)
which fit into the standard commutative diagrams.
A morphism of algebras
(C,T_{C})\to(C^{\prime},T_{C^{\prime}})
is
\varphi\colon C\to C^{\prime}
such that
T_{C^{\prime}}\circ F(\varphi)=\varphi\circ T_{C}
. We denote by
{\mathcal{C}}^{F}
the (Eilenberg-Moore) category of algebras over
F
and algebra homomorphisms.
If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a 2-category, all the equalities should be replaced by isomorphisms.

We denote by
\mathscr{A}b_{r}\subset\mathscr{E}x_{r}
the full 2-subcategory whose objects are abelian categories endowed with the exact structure in which conflations are the short exact sequences.

If
{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathscr{E}x_{r}
is abelian, then
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is its right abelian envelope. In particular,
\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}^{2}({\mathcal{E}})
is an equivalence, for any
{\mathcal{E}}\in\mathscr{E}x_{r}
. We define isomorphism

\mu:=(\mathscr{I}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}})^{-1}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}^{2}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}.

Proposition 4.10
.

The pseudo-functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
together with the pseudo-natural transformations
\mathscr{I}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{E}x_{r}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
,
\mu\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}^{2}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
defines a monad
({\mathcal{A}}_{r},\mathscr{I},\mu)
on the category
\mathscr{E}x_{r}
.
The subcategory
\mathscr{A}b_{r}\subset\mathscr{E}x_{r}
is equivalent to the category
\mathscr{E}x_{r}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}}
of algebras over this monad.

Proof.

It is straightforward to verify that
({\mathcal{A}}_{r},\mathscr{I},\mu)
is a monad.

For
{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathscr{A}b_{r}
, define
T_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{A}})\to{\mathcal{A}}
as the quasi-inverse of
\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{A}})
. Then the pair
({\mathcal{A}},T_{\mathcal{A}})
is an algebra over
({\mathcal{A}}_{r},\mathscr{I},\mu)
and, for
{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{A}b_{r}
, any
\Phi\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathscr{A}b_{r}}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
satisfies
T_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}}\circ{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi)\simeq\Phi\circ T_{{\mathcal{A}}}
, i.e.
\mathscr{A}b_{r}
is a subcategory of
\mathscr{E}x_{r}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}}
.

We check that the inclusion
\mathscr{A}b_{r}\to\mathscr{E}x_{r}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}}
is an equivalence. By definition of an algebra over a monad, for
({\mathcal{E}},T_{{\mathcal{E}}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathscr{E}x_{r}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}}
, the composite
{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{T_{{\mathcal{E}}}}{\mathcal{E}}
is isomorphic to
\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}
. It follows that
\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ T_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}}\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}\circ\mathscr{I}_{{\mathcal{E}}}
. As the composition with
\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}
yields an equivalence
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
, we conclude that
\mathscr{I}_{\mathcal{E}}\circ T_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}
, i.e.
T_{\mathcal{E}}
is an equivalence. In particular,
{\mathcal{E}}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\in\mathscr{A}b_{r}
is abelian.
∎

4.4.
Abelian categories as envelopes for their exact subcategories

The following theorem, based on a result from
[
KS06
]
, is an useful source of examples of abelian envelopes.

Theorem 4.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be an abelian category and
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
a fully exact subcategory closed under kernels of epimorphisms. If every object of
{\mathcal{A}}
is a quotient of an object of
{\mathcal{E}}
then
{\mathcal{A}}
is the right abelian envelope for
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be an abelian category.
According to
[
KS06
, Theorem 8.7.2]
, if
{\mathcal{E}}
is a full subcategory of an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
and every object of
{\mathcal{A}}
is a quotient of an object of
{\mathcal{E}}
, then the embedding functor
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
induces an equivalence of
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}})
with the category of functors
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
which take any one-sided exact sequence
E^{\prime}\to E\xrightarrow{d}E^{\prime\prime}\to 0
with all terms in
{\mathcal{E}}
to a one-sided exact sequence in
{\mathcal{B}}
. We shall check that the latter category is equivalent to
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
. By definition, then
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Let
\Phi\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be a right exact functor. By assumption the kernel
\widetilde{E}
of
d
is an object of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Moreover, the surjective map
E^{\prime}\to\widetilde{E}
has a kernel
E_{1}
which is also an object of
{\mathcal{E}}
. The right exactness of
\Phi
implies that sequences
\Phi(E_{1})\to\Phi(E^{\prime})\to\Phi(\widetilde{E})\to 0
,
\Phi(\widetilde{E})\to\Phi(E)\to\Phi(E^{\prime\prime})\to 0
are exact. Hence so is
\Phi(E^{\prime})\to\Phi(E)\to\Phi(E^{\prime\prime})\to 0
.
∎

This theorem has a nice application to the category of vector bundles. Recall that a noetherian scheme
X
has a
resolution property
if every coherent sheaf on
X
is a quotient of a locally free sheaf.

Corollary 4.12
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme with a resolution property. Then the category
\mathop{\textrm{Coh}}\nolimits(X)
of coherent sheaves is the right abelian envelope for its fully exact subcategory
\textrm{Bun}(X)
of locally free sheaves.

Proof.

The subcategory
\textrm{Bun}(X)\subset\mathop{\textrm{Coh}}\nolimits(X)
is closed under extensions and kernels of epimorphisms. By assumption, every
F\in\mathop{\textrm{Coh}}\nolimits(X)
is a quotient of some
E\in\textrm{Bun}(X)
. We conclude by Theorem
4.11
.
∎

4.5.
Abelian envelopes for additive categories with weak (co)kernels

For an additive category
{\mathcal{C}}
the category
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
of
finitely presented contravariant functors
is the full subcategory of
\mathop{\textrm{Fun}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},{\mathcal{A}}b)
whose objects are functors
\Phi
admitting a presentation
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(-,C_{1})\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(-,C_{2})\to\Phi\to 0
. The Yoneda embedding
{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathop{\textrm{Fun}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},{\mathcal{A}}b)
restricts to a fully faithful functor
h\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
,
C\mapsto h^{C}(-)=\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(-,C)
.

We say that an additive category
{\mathcal{C}}
is
additively generated
by objects
C_{1},\ldots,C_{n}
, if any
C\in{\mathcal{C}}
is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of copies of
C_{i}
.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a category additively generated by
\{C_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Then
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is equivalent to the category of finitely presented right modules over the ring
A:=\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}C_{i})
.

Proof.

Consider the full
\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits
-enriched subcategory
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
on objects
C_{1},\ldots,C_{n}
, i.e. the subcategory together with the structure of abelian groups on
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits
-spaces. By
[
Mac71
, Exercise VIII.2.6(a)]
, there exists a universal additive category
\textrm{Add}({\mathcal{C}}_{0})
.
Functor
\varphi\colon\textrm{Add}({\mathcal{C}}_{0})\to{\mathcal{C}}
, extending the embedding
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{C}}
, is essentially surjective and fully faithful, because
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits
-groups are canonically extended to direct sums. Therefore,
\varphi
is an equivalence.
The statement follows from the equivalences
\textrm{Fun}(\textrm{Add}({\mathcal{C}}_{0}),{\mathcal{A}}b)\simeq\mathscr{F}({\mathcal{C}}_{0},{\mathcal{A}}b)\simeq\textrm{Mod-}A
, for the category
\mathscr{F}({\mathcal{C}}_{0},{\mathcal{A}}b)
of
{\mathcal{A}}b
-enriched functors
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{A}}b
.
∎

Recall that
K\xrightarrow{i}X
is a
weak kernel
of
X\xrightarrow{\varphi}Y
if
\varphi\circ i=0
and for any
f\colon Z\to X
such that
\varphi\circ f=0
there exist (not necessarily unique)
g\colon Z\to K
such that
i\circ g=f
.
Weak cokernels
are defined dually.

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an additive category with weak kernels. Endow
{\mathcal{C}}
with the split exact structure. Then
h\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is the right abelian envelope.

Proof.

The category
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is abelian if and only if
{\mathcal{C}}
has weak kernels. This was essentially proved in
[
Fre66
, Theorem 1.4]
and later formulated in
[
Bel00
, Proposition 4.5]
.

Clearly,
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{B}})
is equivalent to
\textrm{Fun}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{B}})
, for any abelian
{\mathcal{B}}
. As
{\mathcal{C}}\subset\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is projectively generating, the fact follows from Proposition
4.18
below, formulated in a more general set-up. ∎

Proposition 4.15
.

[
Bel00
, Proposition 3.6]

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an idempotent split additive category with weak kernels. Then
{\mathcal{C}}\subset\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is the category of projective objects.

By considering the opposite categories, we get

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an additive category with weak cokernels. Endow
{\mathcal{C}}
with the split exact structure. Then
{\mathcal{C}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is the left abelian envelope.

4.6.
Envelopes in the presence of projective/injective generators

Let
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a projectively generating subcategory of an exact category. Then every
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
admits a
projective presentation
, by which we mean a pair of composable morphisms
P_{1}\xrightarrow{i\circ q^{\prime}}P_{0}\xrightarrow{q}E
given by a choice of two conflations
E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{i}P_{0}\xrightarrow{q}E
and
E^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{i^{\prime}}P_{1}\xrightarrow{q^{\prime}}E^{\prime}
.
Let
P_{1}\xrightarrow{i\circ q^{\prime}}P_{0}\xrightarrow{q}E
,
P^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{j\circ p^{\prime}}P^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{p}F
be projective presentations of objects
E,F\in{\mathcal{E}}
. A
morphism of projective presentations
is a commutative diagram

(28)

\textstyle{P^{\prime}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j\circ p^{\prime}}
\textstyle{P^{\prime}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{P_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\circ q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{P_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}

If
{\mathcal{B}}
is an abelian category and
F\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
a right exact functor, then
F
takes a projective presentation of
E
to an exact sequence
F(P_{1})\to F(P_{0})\to F(E)\to 0
in
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Lemma 4.17
.

cf.
[
Pop73
, Lemma 3.6.1]

Let
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a projectively generating subcategory of an exact category.

(i)

Any morphism
f\colon E\to F
in
{\mathcal{E}}
admits a lift to a morphism of projective presentations (
28
).

(ii)

For an additive functor
\Phi\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
to an abelian category
{\mathcal{B}}
, the induced morphism
\textrm{coker }\Phi(i\circ q^{\prime})\to\textrm{coker }\Phi(j\circ p^{\prime})
does not depend on the choice of
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
.

Proof.

The standard proof for the case when
{\mathcal{E}}
is abelian (
cf.

[
Pop73
, Lemma 3.6.1]
) works for the more general case of an exact
{\mathcal{E}}
as well.
∎

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
\mathcal{P}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a projectively generating subcategory of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
\Gamma\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Fun}}\nolimits(\mathcal{P},{\mathcal{B}})
,
\Gamma(F)=F|_{\mathcal{P}}
, is an equivalence for any abelian category
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proof.

Let
\Phi\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be an additive functor. The quasi-inverse of
\Gamma
is defined by extending
\Phi
to a functor
\widetilde{\Phi}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
defined on an object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
via
\widetilde{\Phi}(E):=\textrm{coker }\Phi(i\circ q^{\prime})
, for a projective presentation
P_{1}\xrightarrow{i\circ q^{\prime}}P_{0}\xrightarrow{q}E
. One easily checks that
\widetilde{\Phi}
is a right exact extension of
\Phi
. The uniqueness is guaranteed by Lemma
4.17
.
∎

We use Propositions
4.14
and
4.18
to construct the right abelian envelope of an exact category with a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
.

Theorem 4.19
.

Assume that a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
of an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
has weak kernels.
Then the functor
h^{\wedge}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
,
h^{\wedge}(E)(-)=\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(-,E)
is the right abelian envelope for
{\mathcal{E}}
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{E}}
is a fully exact subcategory of
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
.

Proof.

Proposition
4.14
implies that the Yoneda embedding
h\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
is the right abelian envelope.

Fix an abelian category
{\mathcal{B}}
and consider the composite
\Upsilon\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits(\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}}),{\mathcal{B}})\xrightarrow{(-)\circ h^{\wedge}}\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})\xrightarrow{(-)\circ i}\mathop{\textrm{Fun}}\nolimits({\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{B}})
, where
i\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is the inclusion functor. By Proposition
4.18
, the second functor is an equivalence. The composite
h^{\wedge}\circ i\colon{\mathcal{P}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
is the Yoneda embedding
h
, hence
\Upsilon(-)=(-)\circ h^{\wedge}\circ i=(-)\circ h
is an equivalence. It follows that the precomposition with
h^{\wedge}
is an equivalence
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits(\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}}),{\mathcal{B}})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
, i.e.
h^{\wedge}
is the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{E}}
.

By Lemma
4.8
, to show that
h^{\wedge}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
is an embedding of a fully exact subcategory, it suffices to check that
h^{\wedge}
is full.
Since
{\mathcal{P}}
is projectively generating, the composite of Yoneda embedding
h\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
with the restriction functor
(-)\circ i\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})\to\mathop{\textrm{Fun}}\nolimits({\mathcal{P}},\mathop{\mathcal{A}\textrm{b}}\nolimits^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
takes values in
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
. The induced functor
{\mathcal{E}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
is
h^{\wedge}
.
Functor
h
is fully faithful by the Yoneda lemma and so is
(-)\circ i
by Proposition
4.18
.
Hence,
h^{\wedge}
is fully faithful too.
∎

Dually, we have

Theorem 4.20
.

Let
\mathcal{I}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be an injectively generating subcategory of an
exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Assume that
\mathcal{I}
has weak cokernels. Then the functor
h^{\vee}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to\textrm{fp}(\mathcal{I}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
,
h^{\vee}(E)(-)=\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E,-)
is the left abelian envelope for
{\mathcal{E}}
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{E}}
is a fully exact subcategory of
\textrm{fp}(\mathcal{I}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
.

Proof.

Injectively generating subcategory
\mathcal{I}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
yields a projectively generating subcategory in
{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
. The statement follows from Theorem
4.19
and formula (
25
).
∎

The following lemma gives a criterion for existence of weak kernels which we shall use for projectively generating subcategories.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a
k
-linear additive category generated by finitely many objects
\{C_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. If the algebra
A_{C}:=\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}C_{i})
is right coherent, then
{\mathcal{C}}
has weak kernels.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.13
,
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\textrm{mod-}A_{C}
. As algebra
A_{C}
is right coherent,
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is abelian.

The essential image of
h\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
consists of projective
A_{C}
-modules and every
A_{C}
-module is a quotient of
h^{C}
, for some
C\in{\mathcal{C}}
. Consider
f\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
. The kernel
K
of
h^{f}\colon h^{C}\to h^{C^{\prime}}
is a quotient of
h^{C_{K}}
, for some
C_{K}\in{\mathcal{C}}
. Then morphism
g\colon C_{K}\to C
whose image under
h
is the composite
h^{C_{K}}\to K\to h^{C}
is a weak kernel of
f
.
∎

Corollary 4.22
.

Consider a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Denote by
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
, respectively
{\mathcal{I}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
, the subcategory of projective, respectively injective, objects. Then
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
, respectively
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{I}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
, is the right, respectively left, abelian envelope for
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

By definition a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
is Hom-finite.
By Proposition
3.8
and Remark
3.9
, category
{\mathcal{E}}
has a projective generator
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}P(\lambda)
and an injective generator
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}I(\lambda)
. Algebras
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}P(\lambda))
and
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}I(\lambda))^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
are finite dimensional, hence coherent. Then Lemma
4.21
implies that
{\mathcal{P}}=\textrm{add}\{P(\lambda)\}
has weak kernels and
{\mathcal{I}}=\textrm{add}\{I(\lambda)\}
has weak cokernels.
The statement follows from Theorems
4.19
and
4.20
.
∎

4.7.
Derived equivalence of
{\mathcal{E}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})

Recall that the derived category
{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})
of a weakly idempotent split exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is the quotient of the homotopy category of complexes
{\mathcal{K}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})
by the full subcategory of acyclic complexes. The following fact is well-known.

Lemma 4.23
.

[
Kel96
, Example 12.2]

Let
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
be a projectively generating subcategory in a weakly idempotent split exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then
{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathcal{K}}^{-}({\mathcal{P}})
.

For an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
with a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
with weak kernels, denote by
Li_{R}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{\sharp}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{\sharp}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
the functor of the bounded (bounded above) derived categories induced by the exact functor
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
(see Theorem
4.19
).

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a weakly idempotent split exact category admitting a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
with weak kernels. Then
Li_{R}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Theorem
4.19
implies that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
.
Since
{\mathcal{P}}
is projectively generating both in
{\mathcal{E}}
and in
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, Lemma
4.23
implies that
{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathcal{K}}^{-}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
.
∎

We leave to the reader the formulation of an analogous statement for exact categories with injectively generating subcategories and bounded below derived categories.

Theorem 4.25
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a weakly idempotent split exact category admitting a projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}
with weak kernels. Assume that
{\mathcal{P}}
is idempotent split and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is of finite global dimension. Then
Li_{R}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
is an equivalence.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.15
, the idempotent split category
{\mathcal{P}}
is equivalent to the category of projective objects in
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})
, which is equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
by Theorem
4.19
. The finite global dimension of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
implies that
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq{\mathcal{K}}^{b}({\mathcal{P}})
.

Let
\textrm{gldim}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})=n
.
We check that any
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
admits a finite projective resolution, i.e. there exists an acyclic complex
0\to P_{n+1}\to P_{n}\to\ldots\to P_{0}\to E\to 0
in
{\mathcal{K}}({\mathcal{E}})
with
P_{i}\in{\mathcal{P}}
. This is not obvious because the acyclicity of a complex
i_{R}(E_{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}})\in{\mathcal{K}}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
does not necessarily imply the acyclicity of
E_{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}\in{\mathcal{K}}({\mathcal{E}})
. Since
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is projectively generating, object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
admits a resolution
P_{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}}=\{P_{n}\to\ldots\to P_{0}\to E\to 0\}
with
P_{i}\in{\mathcal{P}}
. By Theorem
4.19
,
i_{R}({\mathcal{E}})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a fully exact subcategory, hence
i_{R}(P_{\mbox{\boldmath{$\cdot$}}})\in{\mathcal{K}}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
is an acyclic complex with
i_{R}(P_{i})\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
projective. The assumption on the global dimension of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
implies that the kernel
K
of
i_{R}(P_{n})\to i_{R}(P_{n-1})
is projective. Hence,
K\simeq i_{R}(P_{n+1})
, for some
P_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{P}}
(see Proposition
4.15
).
Then
0\to P_{n+1}\to P_{n}\to\ldots\to P_{0}\to E\to 0
is a finite projective resolution of
E
.

It follows that the functor
\iota\colon{\mathcal{K}}^{b}({\mathcal{P}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
is an equivalence. Indeed, Lemma
4.23
implies that
\iota
is fully faithful and the essential surjectivity of
\iota
follows from the existence of finite projective resolutions by the unwinding of complexes.
Then, the equivalence
Li_{R}
of Proposition
4.24
restricts to an equivalence
Li_{R}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
.
∎

The following Lemma ensures that the condition in Theorem
4.25
on category
{\mathcal{P}}
to be idempotent split is verified when
{\mathcal{P}}
is Hom-finite and additively generated by a finite number of objects.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a
k
-linear, Hom-finite additive category additively generated by objects
C_{1},\ldots,C_{n}
. If, for all
i
,
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{C}}(C_{i})
is local, then
{\mathcal{C}}
is idempotent split.

Proof.

Algebra
A_{C}:=\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}C_{i})
is finite dimensional, hence right coherent. By Lemma
4.21
, category
{\mathcal{C}}
has weak kernels. By Theorem
4.19
,
{\mathcal{C}}
is a full subcategory of the Deligne finite category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\textrm{mod-}A_{C}
(see Appendix
A
and Lemma
4.13
). For any
i
, object
C_{i}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{C}})
is indecomposable, as
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{C}}}(C_{i})\simeq\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{C}})}(C_{i})
has no non-trivial idempotent. Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{C}})
is Krull-Schmidt, any direct summand of
C:=\bigoplus C_{i}^{a_{i}}\in{\mathcal{C}}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
C_{i}
’s. As
\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{C}})
is idempotent split,
{\mathcal{C}}
is idempotent split too.
∎

4.8.
Abelian envelopes and admissible subcategories

Here we discuss how the idea of left and right admissible subcategories of Section
2
match the idea of abelian envelopes of this section. Recall that right and left admissible subcategories are in bijection with perpendicular torsion pairs (see Definition
2.5
).

Lemma 4.27
.

Let
\Phi\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
,
\Psi\colon{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{E}}
be 1-morphisms in
\mathscr{E}x_{r}
such that
\Phi
is the left adjoint functor to
\Psi
.
Then
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi)
is left adjoint to
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Psi)
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Psi)
is exact.

Proof.

As
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}
is a pseudo-functor
\mathscr{E}x_{r}\to\mathscr{E}x_{r}
(see Proposition
4.9
), it maps adjoint functors to adjoint functors, i.e.
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Phi)\dashv{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Psi)
.
Functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Psi)
is right adjoint, hence it is left exact. By definition, it is also right exact, which implies that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\Psi)
is exact.
∎

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a perpendicular torsion pair in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Assume that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}})
exist. By Lemma
4.27
, the adjoint pair
\iota_{*}\dashv\iota^{!}
of exact functors (see Theorem
2.11
) yields an adjoint pair
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*})\dashv{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
of functors between the abelian envelopes with
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
exact. We check that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})
is the kernel of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
:

Proposition 4.28
.

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a perpendicular torsion pair in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
.
If

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}})
exist, then so does
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})
, and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta_{*})
identifies it with the colocalising subcategory
{\mathcal{K}}
, the kernel of
