{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
.
The universal functor
i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{K}}
is the unique functor which fits into a commutative square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{R}}

where
\kappa_{*}\colon{\mathcal{K}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
denotes the inclusion.

Proof.

Functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
is exact, as it has left adjoint
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*})
(see Lemma
4.27
). As
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*})
is fully faithful, category
{\mathcal{K}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a colocalising subcategory (see Proposition
A.2
). We denote by
\kappa^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{K}}
the functor left adjoint to
\kappa_{*}
(see Lemma
A.3
).

The composite
{\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{\delta_{*}}{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{i_{R}}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}})
is zero, as
\iota^{!}\circ\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
is. Hence, there exists
i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
such that
\kappa_{*}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}=i_{R}\circ\delta_{*}
. Functor
i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}
is right exact, because
\kappa_{*}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}
is.

Let
\delta^{*}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
be the left adjoint to the inclusion
\delta_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
. As functor
\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{K}}
vanishes on
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
, decomposition (
4
) implies an isomorphism
\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}\simeq\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}\circ\delta_{*}\circ\delta^{*}
. Then,
\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}\simeq\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}\circ\delta_{*}\circ\delta^{*}\simeq\kappa_{*}\circ\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\circ\delta^{*}\simeq i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\circ\delta^{*}
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be an abelian category. We check that
(-)\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{B}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{B}})
is essentially surjective and fully faithful.

For
F\in\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{B}})
, functor
F\circ\delta^{*}
is an object of
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
. Hence, there exist
\overline{F}\in\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{B}})
such that
\overline{F}\circ i_{R}\simeq F\circ\delta^{*}
. Then
\overline{F}\circ\kappa_{*}
is a functor in
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{B}})
such that
\overline{F}\circ\kappa_{*}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq\overline{F}\circ i_{R}\circ\delta_{*}\simeq F\circ\delta^{*}\circ\delta_{*}\simeq F
.

A natural transformation
\nu\colon G_{1}\to G_{2}
of functors in
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{B}})
induces a natural transformation
\nu\circ\kappa^{*}
of functors in
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{B}})
. The composite with
i_{R}
gives a natural transformation
\nu\circ\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}\simeq\nu\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\circ\delta^{*}
of functors in
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
. If
\nu\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}
is zero, then so is
\nu\circ\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}
. As
(-)\circ i_{R}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{B}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
is faithful, natural transformation
\nu\circ\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}
is zero if and only if
\nu\circ\kappa^{*}
is. If it is the case, then
\nu\simeq\nu\circ\kappa^{*}\circ\kappa_{*}
is zero, i.e.
(-)\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{B}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{B}})
is faithful.

Finally, let
\nu\colon G_{1}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\to G_{2}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}
be a natural transformation. It induces a natural transformation
\nu\circ\delta^{*}
of functors in
\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
. As
G_{j}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\circ\delta^{*}\simeq G_{j}\circ\kappa^{*}\circ i_{R}
, for
j=1,2
, fullness of
(-)\circ i_{R}\colon\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{B}})\to\mathop{\textrm{Rex}}\nolimits({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{B}})
implies existence of
\overline{\nu}\colon G_{1}\circ\kappa^{*}\to G_{2}\circ\kappa^{*}
such that
\overline{\nu}\circ i_{R}=\nu\circ\delta^{*}
. Then
\overline{\nu}\circ\kappa_{*}\colon G_{1}\circ\kappa^{*}\circ\kappa_{*}\simeq G_{1}\to G_{2}\simeq G_{2}\circ\kappa^{*}\circ\kappa_{*}
is a natural transformation such that
\overline{\nu}\circ\kappa_{*}\circ i_{R}^{{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq\overline{\nu}\circ i_{R}\circ\delta_{*}\simeq\nu\circ\delta^{*}\circ\delta_{*}\simeq\nu
.
∎

Dually we have:

Proposition 4.29
.

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a perpendicular torsion pair in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
.
If

{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}})
exist, then so does
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{T}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}(\iota_{*})
identifies it with the localising subcategory
{\mathcal{K}}
, the kernel of
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}(\delta^{*})
.
The universal functor
i_{L}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{T}})\simeq{\mathcal{K}}
is the unique functor which fits into a commutative square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{L}^{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{L}}

where
\kappa_{*}\colon{\mathcal{K}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
denotes the inclusion.

The following proposition gives a suitable characterisation of the exact category with a perpendicular torsion pair as a subcategory in its right abelian envelope.

We say that
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
is a perpendicular
torsion pair with right envelopes
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
if the right abelian envelopes
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}})
, and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})
exist.

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
be a perpendicular torsion pair with right envelopes in a
k
-linear exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Assume that
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is full.
Then an object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is isomorphic to
i_{R}(E)
, for some
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
, if and only if

(1)

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta^{*})(A)\simeq i_{R}(F)
, for some
F\in{\mathcal{F}}
,

(2)

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})(A)\simeq i_{R}(T)
, for some
T\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and

(3)

the adjunction counit
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})A\to A
is a monomorphism.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.8
functor
i_{R}
is exact and its essential image is closed under extensions.

An object
E\in{\mathcal{E}}
fits into a conflation
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}E\to E\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}E
. As
i_{R}
is exact,

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})i_{R}(E)\to i_{R}(E)\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta^{*})i_{R}(E)\to 0

is a short exact sequence in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Now let
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
satisfy condition
(1)
and
(2)
.
Then
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta^{*})A\simeq i_{R}(\delta_{*}F)
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})A\simeq i_{R}(\iota_{*}T)
. By Proposition
4.28
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a colocalising subcategory with quotient functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
. By Lemma
A.3
, sequence

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{A}}A\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta^{*})A\to 0

is exact.
By condition
(3)
,
\varepsilon_{A}
is injective, hence
A
is an extension of
i_{R}(\delta_{*}F)\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\delta^{*})A
by
i_{R}(\iota_{*}T)\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*}){\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})A
. Since
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is closed under extensions, we conclude that
A\in{\mathcal{E}}
.
∎

By Proposition
3.5
, a lower ideal
I
in the canonical poset of a thin category gives a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})
.

Proposition 4.31
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a thin category and
\Lambda
its canonical poset. For a lower ideal
I\subset\Lambda
, the perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})

yields an abelian recollement

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\xrightarrow{{i_{I}}_{*}}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{j_{I}^{*}}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{I}).

Proof.

By Proposition
4.28
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a Serre subcategory. Corollary
4.22
and Lemma
4.13
imply that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a Deligne finite category, see Appendix
A.3
. By Proposition
A.9
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is bi-localising, hence it yields an abelian recollement by Proposition
A.5
.
∎

We denote by
Li_{I}^{*}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))\to{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I}))
the derived functor of
i_{I}^{*}
. It exists because
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
has enough projective objects (see Corollary
4.22
).

Lemma 4.32
.

For
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, the kernel
K_{A}
of the adjunction unit
{j_{I}}_{!}j_{I}^{*}A\to A
is isomorphic to
{i_{I}}_{*}L^{1}i_{I}^{*}A
.

Proof.

Let
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
and
0\to B\to P\to A\to 0
an exact sequence with
P\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
projective. Object
{i_{I}}_{*}L^{1}i_{I}^{*}A
is isomorphic to the kernel of
{i_{I}}_{*}i_{I}^{*}B\to{i_{I}}_{*}i_{I}^{*}P
. As, for any
A^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
,
the cokernel of
{j_{I}}_{!}j_{I}^{*}A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
{i_{I}}_{*}i_{I}^{*}A^{\prime}
(see Lemma
A.3
), the snake lemma for

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{j_{I}}_{!}j_{I}^{*}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{j_{I}}_{!}j_{I}^{*}P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{j_{I}}_{!}j_{I}^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

yields an exact sequence
K_{B}\to K_{P}\to K_{A}\to{i_{I}}_{*}{i_{I}}^{*}B\to{i_{I}}_{*}{i_{I}}^{*}P\to{i_{I}}_{*}{i_{I}}^{*}A\to 0
. Projective
P\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
lies in the subcategory
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, hence Proposition
4.30
implies that the adjunction counit
{j_{I}}_{!}j_{I}^{*}P\to P
is injective, i.e.
K_{P}\simeq 0
. Hence,
K_{A}\simeq\textrm{ker }({i_{I}}_{*}{i_{I}}^{*}B\to{i_{I}}_{*}{i_{I}}^{*}P)\simeq{i_{I}}_{*}L^{1}i_{I}^{*}A
.
∎

4.9.
Induced filtrations on abelian envelopes

For an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
, let
\textrm{Serre}({\mathcal{A}})
denote the poset of Serre subcategories in
{\mathcal{A}}
with the inclusion order.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a finite lattice with the minimal element
0
and the maximal element
1
. A
localising
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{Serre}({\mathcal{A}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{A}}_{I}
, such that

(Si)

for any
I\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is a localising subcategory,
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\simeq 0
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}
,

(Sii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{A}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cup J}^{\perp}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{I}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{J}^{\perp}
.

A
colocalising
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
on
{\mathcal{A}}
is a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{Serre}({\mathcal{A}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{A}}_{I}
, such that

(Si)

for any
I\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is a colocalising subcategory,
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\simeq 0
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}
,

(Sii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{A}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
and
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cup J}\simeq{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
.

We say that a map of posets
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{Serre}({\mathcal{A}})
is a
bi-localising

{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration if it is both localising and colocalising.

We say that a (co)localising
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration is
strict
if

(Siii)

for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cup J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{I}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}\oplus{\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}
.

Unlike for exact categories, condition
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{J}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{I}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cup J}
does not imply (Siii):

Example 4.33
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be the category of representations of the quiver with relations:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise
