ba=0,\,bdca=0,\,cd=0,\,cabd=0.

Consider the partial order
\Lambda

with
2\prec 1
and
2\prec 3
. The lattice
{\mathcal{L}}
of lower ideals in
\Lambda
has 5 elements
\emptyset
,
\{2\}
,
\{1,2\}
,
\{2,3\}
,
\{1,2,3\}
ordered by inclusion. The map
{\mathcal{L}}\to\textrm{Serre}({\mathcal{A}})
,
I\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}(S_{i})_{i\in I}
is a bi-localising
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{A}}
(see Proposition
A.9
).

If
I=\{1,2\}
,
J=\{2,3\}
, then
I\cap J=\{2\}
and
I\cup J=\{1,2,3\}
. The category
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{J}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{23}/{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\simeq k\textrm{-vect}
is equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}_{I}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cup J}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{12}\simeq k\textrm{-vect}
, while the quotient
{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cup J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I\cap J}={\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is the category of representations of the quiver

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.54431pt\raise 8.81248pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{ca}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{3\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.66023pt\raise-9.73608pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{bd}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.50002pt\raise-4.30554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\quad bdca=0,\,cabd=0.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be as above. For
I\in{\mathcal{L}}
, let
{\mathcal{L}}/I
be the sublattice of
{\mathcal{L}}
of the elements
J
with
I\prec J
. Then
{\mathcal{L}}/I
is a lattice with the minimal element
I
and the maximal element
1
.

Lemma 4.34
.

Let
\{{\mathcal{A}}_{J}\}
be a strict (co)localising
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{A}}
. Then
\{{\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}\}
is a strict (co)localising
{\mathcal{L}}/I
-filtration on
{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}
.

Proof.

If
{\mathcal{A}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{J}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
are colocalising subcategories, then
{\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}
is a colocalising subcategory,
cf.
Lemma
A.6
. We have:

\displaystyle({\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})\cap({\mathcal{A}}_{K}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})=({\mathcal{A}}_{J}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{K})/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}={\mathcal{A}}_{J\cap K}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}.

By Proposition
A.4
,
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
is identified with
{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
via the functor left adjoint to the quotient functor. Since
({\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})/({\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
, we have
{}^{\perp}({\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})={\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{J}
. Hence,

\displaystyle{}^{\perp}({\mathcal{A}}_{J\cup K}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})={\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{J\cup K}={\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{J}\cap{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{K}={}^{\perp}({\mathcal{A}}_{J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})\cap{}^{\perp}({\mathcal{A}}_{K}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I}).

Equivalence
({\mathcal{A}}_{K\cup J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})/({\mathcal{A}}_{K\cap J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{I})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{K\cup J}/{\mathcal{A}}_{K\cap J}
implies (Siii).
∎

Theorem 4.35
.

Consider a finite lattice
{\mathcal{L}}
and a strict left admissible
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration
\{{\mathcal{F}}_{I}\}
on an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Assume that the exact category
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{J}
has right abelian envelope, for any
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
. Then
\{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\}
is a strict colocalising
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration on
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

For
I\in{\mathcal{L}}
, let
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
. By Proposition
4.28
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a colosalising subcategory with
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})/{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})
. By Proposition
A.4
,
{}^{\perp}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I}))\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{I})
considered as a subcategory of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
via the functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota_{*})
, right adjoint to
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\iota^{!})
, see Lemma
4.27
. As
0\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(0)
, condition (Si) holds.

Propositions
A.4
and
4.28
imply that, for a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{F}})
in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}})={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}})
.

Since the
{\mathcal{L}}
-filtration is strict, Proposition
2.21
implies existence of the perpendicular torsion pairs
({\mathcal{X}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})
,
({\mathcal{X}}_{J},{\mathcal{F}}_{J})
,
({\mathcal{X}}_{I}\oplus{\mathcal{X}}_{J},{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
in
{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J}
.
Hence,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{X}}_{I})^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J})
, similarly for
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{J})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
. Then:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{J})={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{X}}_{I})^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{X}}_{J})^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J})=

\displaystyle={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{X}}_{I}\oplus{\mathcal{X}}_{J})^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J})={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J}).

By Lemma
2.20
, the left dual
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
filtration is also strict. An analogous argument for perpendicular torsion pairs in
{\mathcal{T}}_{I\cap J}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{J}
analogously implies that
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J})={}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{J})
.

Consider
I,J\in{\mathcal{L}}
. By Proposition
4.28
, the quotient
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J})/{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
is equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})={\mathcal{A}}_{r}(({\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})\oplus({\mathcal{F}}_{J}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J}))
, see Proposition
2.21
. As a right exact functor on
({\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})\oplus({\mathcal{F}}_{J}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
is a pair of right exact functors, the category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})\oplus{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{J}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
is the right abelian envelope for
({\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})\oplus({\mathcal{F}}_{J}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
, i.e.
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cup J})/{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})\oplus{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{J}\cap{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{F}}_{I\cap J})
and (Siii) holds.
∎

5.
Highest weight categories as abelian envelopes of thin categories

We recall the definition of a highest weight category and assign two thin categories to it, the subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
of standardly stratified objects and the subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
of costandardly stratified objects. We prove that any highest weight category is the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and the left abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
. Conversely, we prove that the left and right abelian envelopes of any thin category are highest weight categories.

5.1.
Standarizable collections

Let
\Lambda
be a finite poset.
By generalizing the definition of standarizable collections in
[
DR92
]
, we say that a collection
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of objects in a
k
-linear exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
is a
weakly
\Lambda
-standarizable collection
if
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(E(\lambda),E(\mu))=0=\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E(\lambda),E(\mu))
, for
\lambda\succ\mu
or
\lambda
and
\mu
non-comparable. A weakly
\Lambda
-standarizable collection is
\Lambda
-standarizable
if in addition
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E(\lambda),E(\lambda))=k
,
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(E(\lambda),E(\lambda))=0
, for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

Let
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be a weakly
\Lambda
-standarizable collection in an exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. For a lower ideal
I\subset\Lambda
, denote by
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in I})
the extension closure of
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in I}
, similarly denote
{\mathcal{T}}_{I}:={\mathcal{F}}(\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\notin I})
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be a weakly
\Lambda
-standarizable collection in an
exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then,
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
has a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})
, for any lower ideal
I\subset\Lambda
.

Proof.

Clearly
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{I}^{\perp}
.
By Lemma
2.7
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
admits a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})
.
∎

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\Lambda
,
\Lambda^{\prime}
be posets on a finite set.
We say that
\Lambda

dominates

\Lambda^{\prime}
if

\lambda\preceq_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\mu\,\Rightarrow\lambda\preceq_{\Lambda}\mu.

Proposition 5.3
.

Consider a
\Lambda
-standarizable collection
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
in a Hom and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
finite
k
-linear exact category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then the subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
is thin with irreducible objects
\{E(\lambda)\}
and

I\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
is a strict left admissible
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
-filtration on
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1
, category
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
admits perpendicular torsion pairs
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})
, for
I\in{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
. A choice of a full order
\lambda_{1}\prec\lambda_{2}\prec\ldots\prec\lambda_{n}
on
\Lambda
compatible with the poset structure gives lower ideals
I_{i}:=\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{i}\}
. Then

0={\mathcal{T}}_{I_{n}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{n-1}}\subset\ldots\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{0}={\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
is a right admissible filtration. Graded factors
({\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda})^{o}_{i}:={\mathcal{T}}_{I_{i}}^{\perp}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{i-1}}
are equivalent to
{\mathcal{F}}(E(\lambda_{i}))
. As
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E(\lambda_{i}),E(\lambda_{i}))=k
and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(E(\lambda_{i}),E(\lambda_{i}))=0
, the category
({\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda})^{o}_{i}
is equivalent to
k\textrm{-vect}
, hence
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
is thin.
By Lemma
3.3
,
E(\lambda)
’s are the irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
.

The vanishing of
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits
and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
groups in a
\Lambda
-standarizable collection implies that the opposite of the canonical poset
\Lambda_{\textrm{can}}
(
20
) of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
is dominated by the original poset on
\Lambda
, hence a lower ideal in
\Lambda
is a lower ideal in
\Lambda_{\textrm{can}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
. It follows from Proposition
3.5
that
I\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
is a strict left admissible
{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda)
-filtration on
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda}
.
∎

5.2.
From highest weight categories to thin categories and back

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field,
{\mathcal{A}}
a Deligne finite
k
-linear category (see Appendix
A.3
),
and
\Lambda
a partial order on the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
L(\lambda)
denote the irreducible object corresponding to
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
P(\lambda)
its projective cover, and
I(\lambda)
its injective hull. Denote by
\Delta(\lambda)
the
standard object
, i.e. the maximal quotient of
P(\lambda)
with simple factors isomorphic to
L(\mu)
, for
\mu\preceq\lambda
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{A}}
(or simply
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta)
when the partial order is fixed) be the extension closure of the
\Delta(\lambda)
’s.

The partial order
\Lambda
is
adapted
if for any
M\in{\mathcal{A}}
with top
L(\lambda_{1})
and socle
L(\lambda_{2})
, with
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
incomparable, there exists
\mu\in\Lambda
such that
\mu\geq\lambda_{1}
,
\mu\geq\lambda_{2}
and
L(\mu)
is a graded factor of a filtration of
M
(see details in
[
DR92
]
).

We say that
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
is a
highest weight category
(hw category) if, for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
,

(st1)

\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\Delta(\lambda))\simeq k
and

(st2)

P(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
.

By
[
DR92
, Theorem 1]
, if
\Lambda
is adapted conditions (
st1
) and (
st2
) are equivalent to the following more traditional conditions for an hw category:

(
st1^{\prime}
)

there exists an epimorphism
\Delta(\lambda)\to L(\lambda)
whose kernel admits a filtration with graded factors
L(\mu)
, where
\mu\prec\lambda
,

(
st2^{\prime}
)

there exists an epimorphism
P(\lambda)\to\Delta(\lambda)
whose kernel admits a filtration with graded factors
\Delta(\mu)
, where
\mu\succ\lambda
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be a Deligne finite category and a partial order on the set of isomorphism classes of simple objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
. Assume there exist objects
\{\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
which satisfy (
st1^{\prime}
) and (
st2^{\prime}
). Then
\{\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
are the standard objects, hence
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
is an hw category.

Proof.

Let
i_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}(\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\preceq\lambda})\to{\mathcal{A}}
be the inclusion of the extension closure of
\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\preceq\lambda}
and
i^{*}
its left adjoint. Then the standard object
\Delta(\lambda)=i^{*}P(\lambda)
is the projective cover of
L(\lambda)
in
{\mathcal{F}}(\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\preceq\lambda})
, see Lemma
5.5
. We check that
\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)
is a projective object in
{\mathcal{F}}(\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\preceq\lambda})
with the maximal semi-simple quotient
L(\lambda)
. These properties characterise the projective cover of
L(\lambda)
up to an isomorphism, hence
\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)\simeq\Delta(\lambda)
.

By (
st1^{\prime}
),
\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}(\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\preceq\lambda})
. By (
st2^{\prime}
),
\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)
is a quotient of
P(\lambda)
. Hence, for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\overline{\Delta}(\lambda),A)
is a subspace of
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\lambda),A)
. In particular,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\overline{\Delta}(\lambda),L(\mu))\simeq 0
, for
\mu\neq\lambda
, and
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\overline{\Delta}(\lambda),L(\lambda))\simeq k
. It follows that
L(\lambda)
is the maximal semi-simple quotient of
\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)
.

Similarly,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\overline{\Delta}(\mu),L(\nu))\simeq 0
, for
\nu\neq\mu
. Together with (
st2^{\prime}
) it implies that, for the kernel
K
of the surjective map
P(\lambda)\to\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)
and
\mu\leq\lambda
,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(K,L(\mu))=0
. It follows that, for
\mu\preceq\lambda
,
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(\overline{\Delta}(\lambda),L(\mu))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(P(\lambda),L(\mu))\simeq 0
i.e.
\overline{\Delta}(\lambda)
is projective in
{\mathcal{F}}(\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\preceq\lambda})
.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

[
Pop73
, Lemma 3.2.7]

Let
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
be a functor of abelian categories with exact right adjoint
G
. Then
F
maps projective
P\in{\mathcal{A}}
to a projective object in
{\mathcal{B}}
.

If
P
is a projective generator in an hw category
{\mathcal{A}}
, then
{\mathcal{A}}
is equivalent to the category of finite dimensional right modules over the algebra
A:=\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(P)
. Then the pair
(A,\Lambda)
, of a finite dimensional
k
-algebra and a partial order on its simple modules is a
quasi-hereditary algebra

[
CPS88
]
. There is a one-to-one correspondence between Morita equivalence classes of quasi-hereditary algebras and hw categories
[
CPS88
, Theorem 3.6]
. A possible choice for
P
is
P=\oplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}P(\lambda)
, algebra
A=\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}P(\lambda))
is then the unique up to isomorphism basic algebra in the Morita equivalence class.

By
[
PS88
, Theorem 4.3]
, algebra
(A^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits},\Lambda)
is also quasi-hereditary. In particular,
{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\simeq(\textrm{mod-}A)^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\simeq\textrm{mod-}A^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is an hw category. By transporting the standard objects from
{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
to
{\mathcal{A}}
, we get
costandard objects

\nabla(\lambda)
, labelled by
\lambda\in\Lambda
, defined by conditions:

(
cost1
)

there exists an injective morphism
L(\lambda)\to\nabla(\lambda)
whose cokernel admits a filtration with graded factors
L(\mu)
, where
\mu\prec\lambda
,

(
cost2
)

there exists an injective morphism
\nabla(\lambda)\to I(\lambda)
whose cokernel admits a filtration with graded factors
\nabla(\mu)
, where
\mu\succ\lambda
.

\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is a
\Lambda
-standarizable collection in a hw category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
and while
\{\nabla(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is
\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
-standarizable (
cf.

[
DR92
]
).

For an hw category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
, denote by
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{A}}
the extension closure of the
\nabla(\lambda)
’s.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category. Then
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
are thin categories with irreducible objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
, respectively
\{\nabla(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

If
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
is an hw category, then
{\mathcal{A}}
is the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and the left abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
.

Proof.

By (
st2
),
P(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
, for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
. Hence, any object of
{\mathcal{A}}
is a quotient of an object of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
. By
[
Rin91
, Theorem 3]
, category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms. By Theorem
4.11
,
{\mathcal{A}}
is the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
.
∎

5.3.
Envelopes of thin categories are highest weight categories

Consider a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
with the canonical poset
\Lambda
on the set
\{E(\lambda)\}
of irreducible objects in it, as in (
20
). By Corollary
4.22
, the right and left abelian envelopes for
{\mathcal{E}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
, exist.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a thin category.
Then
({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is an hw category with standard objects
\{\Delta(\lambda):=i_{R}(E(\lambda))\}
, and
i_{R}
induces an equivalence
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}})\simeq{\mathcal{E}}
. Also,
({\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}),\Lambda)
is an hw category with costandard objects
\{\nabla(\lambda):=i_{L}(E(\lambda))\}
, and
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})\simeq{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

We shall prove that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is hw with standard objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}
.

With Proposition
3.8
we have constructed indecomposable projective objects
P(\lambda)
in
{\mathcal{E}}
. By Corollary
4.22
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq\textrm{fp}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq\textrm{mod-}A
, where
{\mathcal{P}}=\textrm{add }\{P(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is a projectively generating subcategory and
A:=\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}P(\lambda))
.

Since the Yoneda embedding is fully faithful and
P(\lambda)
and
P(\mu)
are not isomorphic for
\lambda\neq\mu
(see Proposition
3.8
), indecomposable projective
A
-modules
\{P(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
are pairwise non-isomorphic. For
\lambda\in\Lambda
, let
L(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
be the only irreducible
A
-module that allows a cover (i.e. a non-trivial morphism)
P(\lambda)\to L(\lambda)
. Then
\dim_{k}\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}(P(\lambda),L(\mu))=\delta_{\lambda,\mu}
and the number of irreducible factors of
\Delta(\lambda)
isomorphic to
L(\mu)
equals the dimension of
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}(P(\mu),\Delta(\lambda))
.

We check that
\Delta(\lambda)
verify (
st1^{\prime}
) and (
st2^{\prime}
). By Lemma
5.4
it follows that
\Delta(\lambda)
is the standard object.

Since functor
i_{R}
is fully faithful and exact (see Theorem
4.19
and Lemma
4.21
),
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{E}}}(P(\mu),E(\lambda))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})}(P(\mu),\Delta(\lambda))
and Proposition
3.8
implies that the kernel of the surjective map
P(\lambda)\to\Delta(\lambda)
admits a filtration with factors
\Delta(\mu)
, for
\mu\prec\lambda
. Hence, (
st2^{\prime}
) holds.

Now we calculate irreducible factors of
\Delta(\lambda)
.
If
\lambda\notin I_{\mu}
, then
E(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{I_{\mu}}
and
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\mu),E(\lambda))=0
, since
P(\mu)\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{\mu}}
, by Proposition
3.8
.
Therefore, irreducible factor
L(\mu)
may occur in
E(\lambda)
only for
\lambda\preceq\mu
.

Moreover, taking
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(-,E(\lambda))
out of the conflation
K(\lambda)\to P(\lambda)\to E(\lambda)
with
K(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{T}}_{I_{<\lambda}}
(see Proposition
3.8
) implies that
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{E}}(P(\lambda),E(\lambda))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E(\lambda),E(\lambda))=k
, i.e.
\Delta(\lambda)
has one irreducible factor isomorphic to
L(\lambda)
.

\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\Delta(\lambda),L(\mu))\subset\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\lambda),L(\mu))
vanish for
\lambda\neq\mu
,
hence
\Delta(\lambda)
is the only possible irreducible quotient of
P(\lambda)
. Since the other irreducible factors are
L(\mu)
with
\lambda\prec\mu
,
(
st1^{\prime}
) holds.

By Theorem
4.19
,
i_{R}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is an inclusion of fully exact subcategory. Since it maps irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{E}}
to standard objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
,
i_{R}
induces an equivalence
{\mathcal{E}}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}})
.
∎

Corollary 5.9
.

(cf.
[
MS16
, Lemma 7.1]
,
[
Kra17a
]
)
Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category. Then the embeddings
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})\to{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})\to{\mathcal{A}}
induce derived equivalences

(29)

{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda}))\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}})\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})).

Proof.

Category
{\mathcal{A}}
has finite homological dimension
[
PS88
, Theorem 4.3]
. It is the right abelian envelope for thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
(see Propositions
5.6
and
5.7
) and the left abelian envelope for
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
.
By Lemma
3.2
, categories
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
are weakly idempotent split. Projectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{F}}(\{P(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda})
in
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and injectively generating subcategory
{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{F}}(\{I(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda})
in
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
are generated by finitely many objects with local endomorphisms rings (see Proposition
3.8
and Remark
3.9
).
By Lemma
4.26
, both
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{I}}
are idempotent split. By Lemma
4.21
, category
{\mathcal{P}}
has weak kernels while
{\mathcal{I}}
has weak cokernels.
The statement follows from Theorem
4.25
and its dual.
∎

5.4.
An equivalence relation on highest weight structures

An abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
can be hw for different partial orders on isomorphism classes of simples. We introduce the following equivalence relation on the hw structures on
{\mathcal{A}}
:

(30)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)\sim({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textrm{ if }{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})\textrm{ and }{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda^{\prime}})\textrm{ are equal subcategories of }{\mathcal{A}}.

Example 5.10
.

The category
{\mathcal{A}}
of modules over a directed algebra has at least two equivalence classes of hw structures. Indeed, let
\{S_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
be simple objects and
\{P_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
their projective covers such that
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P_{i},P_{j})=0
, for
i<j
. Then for the order
\Lambda:S_{1}\preceq S_{2}\preceq\ldots\preceq S_{n}
,
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is the subcategory of projective objects in
{\mathcal{A}}
. For the order
\Lambda^{\prime}:S_{n}\preceq S_{n-1}\preceq\ldots\preceq S_{1}
,
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda^{\prime}})
is
{\mathcal{A}}
itself.

By Proposition
5.6
, the set
\Lambda
of isomorphism classes of simple objects in an hw category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
is in bijection with the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in thin categories
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
.
This allows us to compare the poset
\Lambda
of
{\mathcal{A}}
with the canonical poset
\Lambda_{\Delta}
of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
, as in (
20
).

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category and
\Lambda_{\Delta}
the canonical poset on the irreducible objects of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
. Then
\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is adapted.

Proof.

For
\lambda\in\Lambda
, let
P(\lambda)
be an indecomposable projective cover of
\Delta(\lambda)
, as constructed in Proposition
3.8
. We write
\lambda<_{c}\mu
, respectively
\lambda\leq_{c}\mu
, when
\lambda
is less than, respectively less than or equal to,
\mu
in the canonical order
\Lambda_{\Delta}
.

By
[
RR23
, Lemma 2.10]
, an order
(\Lambda,>)
is adapted if and only if for any morphism
\varphi\colon P(\lambda)\to P(\mu)
, with
\lambda
and
\mu
not comparable, the image of
\varphi
is contained in
\sum\textrm{im }(\psi\colon P(\nu)\to P(\mu))
, where the sum is taken over all
\nu>\mu
and all
\psi
in
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\nu),P(\mu))
(this is a rephrasing of condition 1 of Lemma 2.10 in
loc.cit.
).

We prove by induction on
|\Lambda|
that any
\varphi\colon P(\lambda)\to P(\mu)
, with
\lambda
and
\mu
not comparable in
\Lambda_{\Delta}
, decomposes as
\sum\psi_{i}\tau_{i}
, for some
\tau_{i}\colon P(\lambda)\to P(\nu_{i})
,
\psi_{i}\colon P(\nu_{i})\to P(\mu)
and
\nu_{i}<_{c}\mu
. The case
|\Lambda|=1
is clear.

For the inductive step, let
\lambda_{m}\in\Lambda_{\Delta}
be a minimal element and let
\varphi
be an element of
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),P(\mu))
with
\lambda
and
\mu
not comparable in the canonical order. We consider three cases:
\lambda=\lambda_{m}
,
\mu=\lambda_{m}
, and
\lambda,\mu\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}
. In the first case,
P(\lambda)=\Delta(\lambda)=\Delta(\lambda_{m})
is the standard object, see Proposition
3.8
. Moreover,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),P(\mu))=0
, as
P(\mu)
admits a filtration with
\Delta(\nu)
for
\nu\leq_{c}\mu
, again by Proposition
3.8
, and
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\Delta(\lambda_{m}),\Delta(\nu))=0
for any
\nu\neq\lambda_{m}
, see (
20
), where
E_{i}
’s must be replaced by
\Delta(\lambda)
’s. Hence,
\varphi=0
has the required decomposition.

If
\mu=\lambda_{m}
and
\mu
and
\lambda
are not comparable in
\Lambda_{\Delta}
then again
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),P(\mu))=0
. Indeed, analogously as above, if
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P(\lambda),P(\mu))=\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),\Delta(\lambda_{m}))\neq 0
then
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\Delta(\nu),\Delta(\lambda_{m}))\neq 0
, for some
\nu\leq_{c}\lambda
. Hence,
\mu=\lambda_{m}\leq_{c}\nu\leq_{c}\lambda
. This contradiction implies that
\varphi
is again the zero morphism, hence it has the required decomposition.

It remains to consider the case
\lambda,\mu\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}
. By Proposition
5.3
, the category
{\mathcal{F}}(\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}})
is thin with irreducible objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}}
. Note that, formula (
20
) implies that the canonical order on the irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{F}}(\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}})
is induced by the embedding
\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}\subset\Lambda
.

The proof of Proposition
3.8
implies that
P(\lambda)
, respectively
P(\mu)
, is the universal extension (
12
) of
Q(\lambda)
, respectively
Q(\mu)
, by
\Delta(\lambda_{m})
, for projective covers
Q(\lambda)
,
Q(\mu)
of
\Delta(\lambda)
and of
\Delta(\mu)
in
{\mathcal{F}}(\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\neq\lambda_{m}})
. In other words,
P(\lambda)
and
P(\mu)
fit into short exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\to\Delta(\lambda_{m})\otimes V_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{i_{\lambda}}P(\lambda)\xrightarrow{d_{\lambda}}Q(\lambda)\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to\Delta(\lambda_{m})\otimes V_{\mu}\xrightarrow{i_{\mu}}P(\mu)\xrightarrow{d_{\mu}}Q(\mu)\to 0,

for vector spaces
V_{\lambda}=\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Q(\lambda),\Delta(\lambda_{m}))^{\vee}
,
V_{\mu}=\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Q(\mu),\Delta(\lambda_{m}))^{\vee}
.

Since
\lambda_{m}\in\Lambda_{\Delta}
is minimal,
\Delta(\lambda_{m})=P(\lambda_{m})
and
\Delta(\nu)\in\Delta(\lambda_{m})^{\perp}
, for any
\nu\neq\lambda_{m}
, see (
20
). In particular,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\Delta(\lambda_{m}),Q(\mu))=0
,
cf.
Proposition
3.8
. Applying functor
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(-,Q(\mu))
to the first short exact sequence implies that the composition with
d_{\lambda}
is an isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Q(\lambda),Q(\mu))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),Q(\mu))
. Hence, for
\varphi\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),P(\mu))
there exists a unique
\overline{\varphi}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Q(\lambda),Q(\mu))
such that
\overline{\varphi}d_{\lambda}=d_{\mu}\varphi
. As
\lambda
and
\mu
are not comparable in the canonical order on
\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}
(see above), the inductive assumption implies that
\overline{\varphi}=\sum\overline{\psi}_{i}\overline{\tau}_{i}
, for some
\overline{\psi}_{i}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Q(\nu_{i}),Q(\mu))
,
\overline{\tau_{i}}\in\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(Q(\lambda),Q(\nu_{i}))
and
\nu_{i}<\mu
in the canonical order on
\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{m}\}
. Since the orders are consistent, we also have
\nu_{i}<_{c}\mu
.

As
P(\nu_{i})
are projective in
{\mathcal{A}}
,
\overline{\psi}_{i}d_{\nu_{i}}
admits a lift to
\psi_{i}\colon P(\nu_{i})\to P(\mu)
, i.e.
\overline{\psi}_{i}d_{\nu_{i}}=d_{\mu}\psi_{i}
. Analogously, we have
\tau_{i}\colon P(\lambda)\to P(\nu_{i})
such that
\overline{\tau}_{i}d_{\lambda}=d_{\nu_{i}}\tau_{i}
. Then

\displaystyle d_{\mu}\sum\psi_{i}\tau_{i}=\sum\overline{\psi}_{i}d_{\nu_{i}}\tau_{i}=\sum\overline{\psi}_{i}\overline{\tau}_{i}d_{\lambda}=\overline{\varphi}d_{\lambda}.

It follows that the difference
\varphi-\sum\psi_{i}\tau_{i}
lies in the kernel
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),\Delta(\lambda_{m})\otimes V_{\mu})
of
d_{\mu}\circ(-)\colon\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),P(\mu))\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P(\lambda),Q(\mu))
, i.e. it decomposes as
P(\lambda)\to\Delta(\lambda_{m})\otimes V_{\mu}=P(\lambda_{m})\otimes V_{\mu}\to P(\mu)
. If
\lambda_{m}<_{c}\mu
, this finishes the proof that
\varphi
factors via
P(\nu_{i})
with
\nu_{i}<_{c}\mu
. If, on the other hand,
\lambda_{m}
is not comparable to
\mu
, then
d_{\mu}\colon P(\mu)\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q(\mu)
is an isomorphism, hence so is
d_{\mu}\circ(-)
. It follows that
\varphi=\sum\psi_{i}\tau_{i}
, as required.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category and

\Lambda_{\Delta}
, respectively
\Lambda_{\nabla}
, the canonical poset on the irreducible objects of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
, respectively
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
. Then
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is hw with the same standard objects, i.e.
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)\sim({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
, and
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda_{\nabla})
is hw with the same costandard objects
\nabla(\lambda)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.7
,
{\mathcal{A}}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda}))
. By Theorem
5.8
,
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is an hw category with standard objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
. Hence,
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)\sim({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
.
∎

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category and

\Lambda_{\Delta}
the canonical poset structure on the irreducible objects of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
. Then
\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is dominated by
\Lambda
.

Proof.

As simple factors
L(\lambda)
of
\Delta(\mu)
satisfy
\lambda\preceq\mu
and
L(\lambda)
is the maximal semi-simple quotient of
\Delta(\lambda)
,

\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\Delta(\lambda),\Delta(\mu))\neq 0
implies
\lambda\preceq\mu
. If
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}(\Delta(\lambda),\Delta(\mu))\neq 0
, then
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(K(\lambda),\Delta(\mu))\neq 0
, for the kernel
K(\lambda)
of the epimorphism
P(\lambda)\to\Delta(\lambda)
in (
st2^{\prime}
). Hence,
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\Delta(\nu),\Delta(\mu))\neq 0
, for some
\nu\succ\lambda
, i.e.
\mu\succeq\nu\succ\lambda
. In other words,

(31)

\Delta(\mu)\notin\Delta(\lambda)^{\perp}\,\Rightarrow\,\lambda\preceq\mu.

In view of definition (
20
), this implies
that
\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is dominated by
\Lambda
.
∎

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category and

\Lambda_{\Delta}
the canonical poset on the irreducible objects of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
. For another partial order
\Lambda^{\prime}
,
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda^{\prime})
is an hw structure equivalent to
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
if and only if
\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is dominated by
\Lambda^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since
\Lambda_{\Delta}
depends only on the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
, Proposition
5.13
implies that if
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)\sim({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda^{\prime})
then
\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is dominated by
\Lambda^{\prime}
.

By Proposition
5.12
,
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
are the standard objects for
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
. In particular, they satisfy (
st1^{\prime}
) and (
st2^{\prime}
). If
\Lambda_{\Delta}^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is dominated by
\Lambda^{\prime}
, then
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
satisfy (
st1^{\prime}
) and (
st2^{\prime}
) for
\Lambda^{\prime}
. Hence,
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda^{\prime})
is hw with standard objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
, by Lemma
5.4
, i.e.
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)\sim({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda^{\prime})
.
∎

5.5.
Characterization of a thin category in its right envelope

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a thin category and
\Lambda
its canonical poset.
A lower ideal
I\subset\Lambda
yields a perpendicular torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}_{I},{\mathcal{F}}_{I})
on
{\mathcal{E}}
(see Proposition
3.5
). Category
{\mathcal{F}}_{I}
is thin, hence
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})
exists and is a Deligne finite category (see Corollary
4.22
). It follows from Propositions
4.28
and
A.9
that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a bi-localising subcategory.

The category
({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is hw (see Theorem
5.8
). In particular, the elements of
\Lambda
are in bijection with isomorphism classes of simple objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Lemma 5.15
.

The category
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is the full subcategory of objects whose simple factors are
L(\lambda)
, for
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus I
.

Proof.

Condition (
st1^{\prime}
) implies that, for any
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus I
,
L(\lambda)
is a quotient of an object in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})
, hence
L(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})
. As
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a subcategory, for any
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus I
,
L(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})
is simple and, for distinct
\lambda,\mu\in\Lambda\setminus I
, objects
L(\lambda)
and
L(\mu)
are not isomorphic.

On the other hand,
({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I}),(\Lambda\setminus I)^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is an hw category (see Theorem
5.8
). In particular, there are
|\Lambda\setminus I|
non-isomorphic simple objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})
. Hence,
\{L(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus I}
is the set of isomorphism classes of simple objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{I})
.
∎

For
\lambda\in\Lambda
, the complement
\Lambda\setminus I_{\lambda}
of the principal ideal
I_{\lambda}\subset\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
is a lower ideal in
\Lambda
. We denote by
({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda},{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda})
the corresponding perpendicular torsion pair (see Proposition
3.5
) and by
{\iota_{\lambda}}_{*}\colon{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}\to{\mathcal{E}}
,
{\delta_{\lambda}}_{*}\colon{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda}\to{\mathcal{E}}
the inclusion functors.

Denote
{\alpha_{\lambda}}_{*}={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\delta_{\lambda}}_{*})\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda})\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\iota_{\lambda}}_{*})\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda})\to{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
.
By Proposition
4.28
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is a Serre subcategory,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})/{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda})
, and, by Lemma
4.27
, the right adjoint
\beta_{\lambda}^{*}
to
{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}
is the quotient functor
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\to{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda})
. Note that, by Lemma
5.15
, the set of isomorphism classes of simple objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda})
is
\{L(\mu)\}_{\mu\in I_{\lambda}}
.

Theorem 5.16
.

Consider a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
with the canonical poset
\Lambda
. Then an object
A
in
{\mathcal{A}}:={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is in the subcategory
{\mathcal{E}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
if and only if, for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
, the adjunction counit
\varepsilon_{\lambda}\colon{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda}^{*}A\to A
is a monomorphism.

Proof.

Since
({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda},{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda})
is a perpendicular torsion pair in
{\mathcal{E}}
, Proposition
4.30
implies that if
A\in{\mathcal{E}}
, then
{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\lambda}}A
is a monomorphism.

We prove the inverse by induction on the number
|\Lambda|
of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{E}}
. The case
|\Lambda|=1
is clear.

Consider a maximal
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
. Category
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}:={\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0}})
is hw with poset
\Lambda^{\prime}:=\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda_{0}\}
. It follows from the definition (
20
) that
\Lambda^{\prime}
is the canonical poset of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0}}
.

Take
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\varepsilon_{\lambda}
is mono on
A
, for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
. We check that
A\in{\mathcal{E}}
.
Proposition
4.30
implies that
A\in{\mathcal{E}}
if and only if
\alpha_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}}
,
\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0}}
and the adjunction counit
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\lambda_{0}}}A
is mono. The first condition is obvious as
{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}}\simeq k\textrm{-vect}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}})
and the third condition holds by the assumption.
Therefore, it suffices to check that
\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0}}
.

By inductive hypothesis it suffices to check that, for any
\mu\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, the adjunction counit
{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}^{*}\beta_{\lambda}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}}\beta_{\lambda}^{*}A
is mono. Here, for the principal ideal
I^{\prime}_{\mu}\subset(\Lambda^{\prime})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
generated by
\mu
, we denote by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu}:={\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda^{\prime}\setminus I^{\prime}_{\mu}}
the right admissible subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0}}
, by
{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu})\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
the inclusion and by
{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}^{*}
its right adjoint.

If
\lambda_{0}\succ\mu
, then
\Lambda\setminus I_{\mu}=\Lambda^{\prime}\setminus I^{\prime}_{\mu}
, hence
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{\mu}
. The composite
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu})\xrightarrow{{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\beta_{\lambda}}_{!}}{\mathcal{A}}
is
{\beta_{\mu}}_{!}
. The
{\beta_{\mu}}_{!}\dashv\beta_{\mu}^{*}
adjunction counit
\varepsilon_{\mu}
decomposes as
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}^{*}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}}{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\lambda_{0}}}A
. Since
\varepsilon_{\mu}
is a monomorphism when applied to
A
, so is
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}
when applied to
\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A
.

If
\lambda_{0}
and
\mu
are not comparable, then
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu}={\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda\setminus(I_{\mu}\cup\{\lambda_{0}\})}
. The inclusion
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}\mu}}_{!}
of the subcategory
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu})\subset{\mathcal{A}}
decomposes as
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu})\xrightarrow{{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}}{\mathcal{A}}
. The category
{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}\mu}:={\mathcal{F}}_{\Lambda\setminus(I_{\mu}\cup\{\lambda_{0}\})}
is a direct sum of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu}
. Hence,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}\mu})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}})\oplus{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mu})
and, in the recollement

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}\mu})\xrightarrow{{\alpha_{\lambda_{0}\mu}}_{*}}{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{{\beta_{\lambda_{0}\mu}}^{*}}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\mu})

functor
{\alpha_{\lambda_{0}\mu}}_{*}
is a direct sum of
{\alpha_{\lambda_{0}}}_{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\lambda_{0}})\to{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\alpha_{\mu}}_{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mu})\to{\mathcal{A}}
. It follows that the derived functor
L\alpha_{\lambda_{0}\mu}^{*}
is a direct sum of
L\alpha_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}
and
L\alpha_{\mu}^{*}
. In view of Lemma
4.32
, the injectivity of
\varepsilon_{\lambda_{0}}
and
\varepsilon_{\mu}
applied to
A
implies
L^{1}\alpha_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A=0=L^{1}\alpha_{\mu}^{*}A
. Hence,
L^{1}\alpha_{\lambda_{0}\mu}^{*}A=0
and
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}\mu}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda_{0}\mu}^{*}A\to A
is a monomorphism (see Lemma
4.32
). As above, the adjunction counit
\varepsilon_{\lambda_{0}\mu}
decomposes as
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}^{*}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}}{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\lambda_{0}}}A
. Hence, the injectivity of
\varepsilon_{\lambda_{0}\mu}
applied to
A
implies that
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}
is injective when applied to
\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A
.

Since
{\mathcal{A}}
has enough projective objects, in either case the injectivity of
{\beta_{\lambda_{0}}}_{!}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}
applied to
\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A
implies the injectivity of
{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}_{!}{\beta^{\prime}_{\mu}}^{*}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mu}}\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A
(see Lemma
A.7
below). By inductive hypothesis,
\beta_{\lambda_{0}}^{*}A
is an object in
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. It follows that
A\in{\mathcal{E}}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.17
.

Conditions (
st1
) and (
st2
) define highest weight categories over arbitrary field
k
. If in the definition of the standarizable collection we require that
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(E_{i},E_{i})
is a division
k
-algebra, then all statements in Section
5
remain true for the more general definition of a thin category (see Remark
3.6
). The proofs go
mutatis mutandis
.

6.
Ringel duality

We present the classical Ringel duality (see
[
Rin91
]
) of hw categories as a duality between the left and right abelian envelope. We define Ringel duality for thin categories.

6.1.
Ringel duality for highest weight categories

An hw category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
is the right abelian envelope of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
and the left abelian envelope of the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
(see Proposition
5.7
). Let
T=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda}T(\lambda)\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
be the direct sum of the injective hulls of
\Delta(\lambda)

(see Remark
3.9
). Object
T
is inductively constructed by
universal extensions
from the irreducible objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}
in
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}
, see Proposition
3.7
. Hence, by
[
DR92
]
,
T\in{\mathcal{A}}
is the
characteristic tilting object
introduced by M. Ringel in
[
Rin91
]
. The algebra
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(T)
is the
Ringel dual
of
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P)
, for a projective generator
P\in{\mathcal{A}}
,
[
Rin91
]
. Both algebras
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P)
and
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(T)
are quasi-hereditary, hence the duality is between quasi-hereditary algebras.

We define Ringel duality as a duality between hw categories.

Definition 6.1
.

Given a highest weight category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
its
Ringel dual
is the hw category
(\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}}),\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
, where
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})={\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda}))
is the left abelian envelope of the thin subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{A}}
.

Since
{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
are the right and left abelian envelopes of the same thin category
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
, we can think about Ringel duality as a transfer from the right to left envelope.

The category
{\mathcal{T}}
additively generated by
\{T(\lambda)\}
is injectively generating in
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
.

Derived equivalences (
29
) together with Proposition
3.4
imply that
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}})
admits two full exceptional sequences
(\nabla(\lambda_{n}),\ldots,\nabla(\lambda_{1}))
and
(\Delta(\lambda_{1}),\ldots,\Delta(\lambda_{n}))
, for any choice of a full order
\lambda_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\lambda_{n}
on
\Lambda
compatible with the poset structure. In particular,
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}})
admits the
Serre functor

\mathbb{S}
(see
[
BK89
, Proposition 3.8]
).

Let
(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n})
be a full exceptional sequence in a triangulated category
{\mathcal{D}}
. The
left dual
exceptional sequence
(F_{n},\ldots,F_{1})
is characterised by the property

(32)

\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{D}}}(E_{i},F_{j}[l])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}k,&\textrm{if }l=0,i=j,\\
0,&\textrm{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Collection
(E_{1},\ldots,E_{n})
is said to be
right dual
to
(F_{n},\ldots,F_{1})

[
Bon89
]
.

Lemma 6.2
.

[
Kra17b
, Lemma 2.1]

Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
be an hw category. Then, for any choice of a full order
\lambda_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\lambda_{n}
on
\Lambda
compatible with the poset structure, the exceptional sequence
(\nabla(\lambda_{n}),\ldots,\nabla(\lambda_{1}))
is left dual to the collection
(\Delta(\lambda_{1}),\ldots,\Delta(\lambda_{n}))
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Consider an hw category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
.

(1)

The category
\bf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
is equivalent to the right abelian envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
.

(2)

Category
\bf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
has costandard objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}
and standard objects
\{\mathbb{S}^{-1}\nabla(\lambda)\}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.13
and Corollary
4.22
,
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})\simeq(\textrm{mod-}\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(T)^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\simeq(\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(T)\textrm{-mod})^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits}\simeq\textrm{mod-}\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(T)
. By
[
DR92
]
,
T
is a projective generator for
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
. It follows from Lemma
4.13
and Corollary
4.22
that
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda}))\simeq\textrm{mod-}\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(T)\simeq\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
.

Let
\Lambda_{\Delta}
be the canonical poset of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
. By Theorem
5.8
,
(\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}}),\Lambda_{\Delta})
is hw with costandard objects
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}
. It follows from Proposition
5.14
that
(\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}}),\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is hw.

Derived equivalence (
29
) implies that
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}}))\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda}))\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}})
. Lemma
6.2
applied to category
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
implies that standard objects in
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
form the collection right dual to
\{\Delta(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
. Then, isomorphisms
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathbb{S}^{-1}(\nabla(\mu)),\Delta(\lambda))\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\Delta(\lambda),\nabla(\mu))
imply that
\{\mathbb{S}^{-1}\nabla(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
are the standard objects in
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
.
∎

It is instructive to encode relations between various relevant categories in the diagram:

All the categories in the picture are considered as full subcategories in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}})
. The categories in the upper row are additive and in the lower row are exact, while
{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathbf{RD}({\mathcal{A}})
are abelian. Furthermore, category
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})\cap{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
(
cf.

[
Rin91
, Theorem 5]
) is a projectively generating subcategory in
{\mathcal{F}}(\nabla_{\Lambda})
and an injectively generating subcategory in
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta_{\Lambda})
. Similar facts hold when shifting along the diagram.

6.2.
Ringel duality for thin categories

The following fact, whose proof is basically due to Ringel, gives a characterization of a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
inside its right abelian envelope.

Proposition 6.4
.

[
Rin91
, Corollary 4]

Let
I\in{\mathcal{E}}
be an injective generator for a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then

{\mathcal{E}}=\{X\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\,|\,\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{k}(X,I)=0,\textrm{ for }k\neq 0\}.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda
be the canonical poset of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Category
({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
is hw with
{\mathcal{E}}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta)
(see Theorem
5.8
). Hence,
I\in{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
is the characteristic tilting module for
({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}),\Lambda^{\mathop{\textrm{op}}\nolimits})
. The statement follows from the description
{\mathcal{F}}(\Delta)=\{X\in{\mathcal{A}}\,|\,\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{k}(X,T)=0,\textrm{ for }k\neq 0\}
, for a characteristic tilting module
T
in a hw category
({\mathcal{A}},\Lambda)
(see
[
Rin91
, Corollary 3]
).
∎

Theorem 6.5
.

A thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
is the intersection
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.22
, an injective generator
I\in{\mathcal{E}}
is an injective generator for
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
. In view of the equivalences (
29
), the statement follows from Proposition
6.4
.
∎

Definition 6.6
.

The
Ringel dual

\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}})
of a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
is the intersection in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
:

\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}})={\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})\cap\mathbb{S}^{-1}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}).

Proposition 6.7
.

The Ringel dual of a thin category is a thin category. We have a canonical equivalence of exact categories:
\bf{RDT}(\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda
be the canonical poset of
{\mathcal{E}}
and
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible objects in
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Category
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is hw with costandard objects
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
(see Theorem
5.8
). Let
\{G(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
denote the exceptional sequence right dual to
\{E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
. Then
\{G(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
are standard objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
(see Lemma
6.2
). Denote by
{\mathcal{F}}_{G}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
their extension closure. The category
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})
, Ringel dual to
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
, is hw with costandard objects
\{G(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
and standard objects
\{\mathbb{S}^{-1}E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
(see Theorem
6.3
). In particular, the category of objects with a standard filtration in
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})
is
\mathbb{S}^{-1}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Derived equivalence (
29
) implies that projective generator
P\in{\mathcal{E}}
is a projective generator for the heart
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
of a
t
-struc
ture on
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
. Similarly, as
\{\mathbb{S}^{-1}E(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
are standard objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})
,
\mathbb{S}^{-1}(P)
is a projective generator for the heart
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})
of a
t
-struc
ture on
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
. It follows that
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})=\mathbb{S}^{-1}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
as subcategories of
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}({\mathcal{E}})
.
As
\{G(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is the set of isomorphism classes of standard objects in
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})
(see Proposition
5.7
). Hence,
\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}})={\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})\cap\mathbb{S}^{-1}{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{F}}_{G})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{G}
(see Theorem
6.5
). Thus, the thin category
{\mathcal{F}}_{G}
(see Proposition
5.3
) is the Ringel dual of
{\mathcal{E}}
.

As
\mathbb{S}^{-1}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))
is the left envelope of
{\mathcal{F}}_{G}
, the double Ringel dual of
{\mathcal{E}}
is
\bf{RDT}(\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}))=\mathbb{S}^{-1}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))\cap\mathbb{S}^{-1}({\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}))
. It follows from Theorem
6.5
that the inverse of the Serre functor induces an equivalence of
{\mathcal{E}}
and its double Ringel dual.
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

For a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
we have canonical equivalences:

(33)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq\bf{RD}({\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}),

(34)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{l}(\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq\bf{RD}({\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}}).

Proof.

It follows form the proof of Proposition
6.7
that, for a thin category
{\mathcal{E}}
,
\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}})
is the thin category of objects with a standard filtration in
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
. Hence,
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(\bf{RDT}({\mathcal{E}}))\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
by Proposition
5.7
. Since
{\mathcal{E}}
is the category of objects with a standard filtration in
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
, (see Theorem
5.8
) category
{\mathcal{A}}_{l}({\mathcal{E}})
is, by Definition
6.1
, the Ringel dual of
{\mathcal{A}}_{r}({\mathcal{E}})
. Equivalences (
33
) follow. Applying the Ringel duality
\bf{RD}
them gives equivalences (
34
).
∎

Appendix A
Abelian categories

In this appendix we gather facts about abelian categories that we use in the main body of the paper. We discuss localising and co-localising subcategories and abelian recollements. We introduce Deligne finite categories, and discuss their Serre subcategories.

A.1.
(Co)localising subcategories

A full subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of an abelian category
{\mathcal{A}}
is a
Serre subcategory
if
{\mathcal{B}}
is closed under extensions, subobjects and quotients. It follows that
{\mathcal{B}}
is abelian, too.

Given a Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
,

S=\{f\in\textrm{Mor}({\mathcal{A}})\,|\,\ker f,\textrm{coker }f\in{\mathcal{B}}\}

is a multiplicative system. The
quotient category

{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is the localisation of
{\mathcal{A}}
in
S
.

The category
{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is abelian and the quotient functor
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is exact. Moreover,
j^{*}
is the universal exact functor
F\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to an abelian category
{\mathcal{C}}
such that
F(B)\simeq 0
, for any
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

A Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is
localising
(
colocalising
) if
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
admits a right (resp. left) adjoint.
A Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is
bi-localising
if it is both localising and colocalising.

Lemma A.1
.

[
Pop73
, Proposition 4.4.3]

Let
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
be a localising, respectively colocalising, subcategory. Then the right, respectively left, adjoint to the quotient functor
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is fully faithful.

We will focus on colocalising subcategories. The dual statements are left to the reader.

Proposition A.2
.

[
Pop73
, Theorem 4.4.9]

Let
T\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
be an exact functor between abelian categories with fully faithful left adjoint. Then
\ker T\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is a colocalising subcategory and
T
induces an equivalence
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}/\ker T
.

For a Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
, the existence of left adjoint to the quotient functor implies the existence of left adjoint to the embedding
{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
:

Lemma A.3
.

Let
i_{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}
be an embedding of a colocalising subcategory,
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
the quotient functor and
j_{!}
its left adjoint. Then
i_{*}
admits the left adjont functor
i^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and adjunction morphisms
fit into the exact sequence

(35)

j_{!}j^{*}A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{A}}A\xrightarrow{\eta_{A}}i_{*}i^{*}A\to 0.

Proof.

Fully faithfulness of
j_{!}
(see Lemma
A.1
) implies that
j^{*}(\varepsilon_{A})\colon j^{*}j_{!}j^{*}A\xrightarrow{\simeq}j^{*}A
is an isomorphism. As
j^{*}
is exact, it follows that
j^{*}(\textrm{coker }\varepsilon_{A})=0
, i.e.
i_{*}i^{*}A\simeq\textrm{coker }\varepsilon_{A}
is indeed an object of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\simeq\ker j^{*}
.

We define the functor
i^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
as the cokernel of
\varepsilon\colon j_{!}j^{*}\to\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}
and check that it is left adjoint to
i_{*}
. Applying
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(-,i_{*}B)
to (
35
) gives an exact sequence:

0\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(i_{*}i^{*}A,i_{*}B)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(A,i_{*}B)\to\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(j_{!}j^{*}A,i_{*}B)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}(j^{*}A,j^{*}i_{*}B)\simeq 0,

hence the isomorphism
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}(i^{*}A,B)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}(i_{*}i^{*}A,i_{*}B)\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits(A,i_{*}B)
.
∎

By
[
Gab62
]
, the perpendicular category to a colocalising subcategory (closed objects) is equivalent to the quotient category. The following proposition, used in Section
4.9
, assures that a colocalising subcategory coincides with its double perpendicular.

Proposition A.4
.

Let
i_{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}
be an embedding of a colocalising subcategory and
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
the quotient functor. Then

(1)

i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is a torsion-free part of a torsion pair,

(2)

{}^{\perp}(i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1})\simeq j_{!}({\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1})
,

(3)

(j_{!}({\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1}))^{\perp}\simeq i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.

Proof.

By
[
BR07
, Proposition 1.2]
,
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is a torsion-free part of a torsion pair if and only if the embedding functor
i_{*}
has left adjoint and, for any exact sequence

(36)

0\to i_{*}A_{1}\to A\to i_{*}A^{\prime}_{1}

in
{\mathcal{A}}
, object
A
lies in the subcategory
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
. By Lemma
A.3
, functor
i^{*}
, left adjoint to
i_{*}
, exists. By applying the exact functor
j^{*}
to (
36
), we get
j^{*}(A)\simeq 0
, i.e.
A\in i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.

By
[
Gab62
]
, the image of
j_{!}\colon{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is
{}^{\perp}(i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1})
. In particular,
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\subset(j_{!}({\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1}))^{\perp}
. For
A\in(j_{!}({\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{A}}_{1}))^{\perp}
the adjunction counit
j_{!}j^{*}A\to A
is zero, hence the exact sequence (
35
) implies that
A\simeq i_{*}i^{*}A
.
∎

A.2.
Abelian recollements and bi-localising subcategories

An
abelian recollement

[
Kuh94
]
is a diagram of abelian categories and additive functors

(37)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}

such that
i^{*}\dashv i_{*}\dashv i^{!}
,
j_{!}\dashv j^{*}\dashv j_{*}
, functors
i_{*}
,
j_{*}
,
j_{!}
are fully faithful, and
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
is the kernel of
j^{*}
.

Given an abelian recollement (
37
), the subcategory
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
is bi-localising. The converse is also true, i.e. every bi-localising subcategory yields a recollement:

Proposition A.5
.

The data of an abelian recollement (
37
) is equivalent to the data of a bi-localising subcategory
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.

Proof.

Consider abelian recollement (
37
). Functor
j^{*}
has a fully faithful right adjoint, hence it is the quotient functor by its kernel
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
(see Proposition
A.2
). As
j^{*}
has also the left adjoint, category
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
is both localising and colocalising.

Let now
i_{*}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
be an embedding of a bi-localising subcategory. The quotient functor
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
has right and left adjoints
j_{*},j_{!}\colon{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
. By
[
Pop73
, Proposition 4.4.3]
, functors
j_{*}
and
j_{!}
are fully faithful. By Lemma
A.3
, functor
i_{*}
has left adjoint
i^{*}
.
Similarly, define
i^{!}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
as the kernel of the
j^{*}\dashv j_{*}
adjunction unit. An analogous argument as in the proof of Lemma
A.3
shows that
i_{*}\dashv i^{!}
.
∎

Adjoint functors for a 2-step filtration with bi-localising subcategories are compatible:

Lemma A.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
be bi-localising subcategories.
Consider the commutative diagram of inclusions and quotients:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{C}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{l^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{o_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{k_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}/{\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{*}}

Then
{\mathcal{C}}/{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is a bi-localising subcategory and functors
j_{!}\circ r_{*}\simeq k_{*}p_{!}
are canonically isomorphic.

Proof.

A straightforward verification shows that
j^{*}l_{!}\dashv s^{*}\dashv j^{*}l_{*}
, i.e.
{\mathcal{C}}/{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is a bi-localising subcategory.

As
l^{*}j_{!}r_{*}\simeq s^{*}j^{*}j_{!}r_{*}\simeq s^{*}r_{*}\simeq 0
, functor
j_{!}r_{*}
takes values in the subcategory
{\mathcal{C}}
. Hence,
j_{!}r_{*}\simeq k_{*}k^{*}j_{!}r_{*}
. The isomorphism
j^{*}k_{*}\simeq r_{*}p^{*}
implies an isomorphism of left adjoint functors
k^{*}j_{!}\simeq p_{!}r^{*}
. Hence,
j_{!}r_{*}\simeq k_{*}k^{*}j_{!}r_{*}\simeq k_{*}p_{!}r^{*}r_{*}
. Since
r_{*}
is fully faithful, the
r^{*}\dashv r_{*}
adjunction counit yields an isomorphism
j_{!}r^{*}\simeq k_{*}p_{!}r^{*}r_{*}\simeq k_{*}p_{!}
.
∎

In Section
5

we use the following fact.

Lemma A.7
.

Consider a recollement (
37
) of abelian categories.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
have enough projectives. Then morphism
\varphi
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
is a monomorphism if
j_{!}(\varphi)
is.

Proof.

Functor
j_{!}
is right exact. As
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
has enough projectives, there exist the derived functor
Lj_{!}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{2})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}})
. As
j^{*}
is exact, it can be derived to a
t
-exact functor
j^{*}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}})\to{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{2})
, i.e. to a functor which preserves the subcategories of complexes concentrated in negative and positive degrees. Since
j^{*}\circ j_{!}\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}
, we have
j^{*}\circ Lj_{!}\simeq\mathop{\textrm{Id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{-}({\mathcal{A}}_{2})}
. In particular,
j^{*}L^{1}j_{!}A=0
, for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. It follows that
L^{1}j_{!}A\in i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
, for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
.

Let
\varphi\colon A^{\prime}_{2}\to A_{2}
be a morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. Denote by
K
its kernel and by
I
its image. If
j_{!}(\varphi)
is a monomorphism, then the map
\alpha
in the exact sequence

L^{1}j_{!}I\to j_{!}K\xrightarrow{\alpha}j_{!}A^{\prime}_{2}\to j_{!}I\to 0

is zero. It follows that
j_{!}K
is a quotient of an object in
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
. As
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
is a kernel of an exact functor
j^{*}
, it is closed under quotients. It follows that
j_{!}K\in i_{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{1})
.
Then
K=j^{*}j_{!}K=0
, i.e.
\varphi
is a monomorphism.
∎

A.3.
Serre subactegories in abelian categories of finite length

We say that a
k
-linear abelian category is
of finite length
if it is
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits
and
\mathop{\textrm{Ext}}\nolimits^{1}
-finite, contains finitely many non-isomorphic simple objects, and any object admits a finite filtration with simple graded factors.

Serre subcategory of a finite length category is determined by simple objects in it.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be an abelian category of finite length and
\mathcal{S}
the set of simple objects in it. Then Serre subcategories in
{\mathcal{A}}
are in one-to-one correspondence with subsets of
{\mathcal{S}}
. Given
I\subset\mathcal{S}
, the corresponding Serre subcategory is the extension closure of
\{S\}_{S\in I}
.

Proof.

Given
I\subset\mathcal{S}
, let
{\mathcal{F}}(S_{i})_{i\in I}
be the full subcategory of objects whose all simple factors are isomorphic to
S_{i}
, for some
i\in I
. It is standard that
{\mathcal{F}}(S_{i})_{i\in I}
is a Serre subcategory. Moreover, any Serre subcategory is of this form. Indeed, given Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
, let
I_{{\mathcal{B}}}\subset{\mathcal{S}}
be the set of simples in
{\mathcal{A}}
which lie in
{\mathcal{B}}
. Then
{\mathcal{F}}(S)_{i\in I_{{\mathcal{B}}}}\subset{\mathcal{B}}
. On the other hand,
{\mathcal{B}}
is closed under subquotients, hence together with an object
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
it contains all its simple factors in
{\mathcal{A}}
, i.e.
B\in{\mathcal{F}}(S)_{i\in I_{{\mathcal{B}}}}
.
∎

An abelian category of finite length is a
Deligne finite category
if it has a projective generator.
By
[
Del90
, Propostion 2.14]
, a Deligne finite category
{\mathcal{A}}
with projective generator
P
is equivalent to the category of finitely generated modules over the (finite dimensional)
k
-algebra
\mathop{\textrm{End}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}(P)
. In particular, Deligne finite categories are Krull-Schmidt.

Proposition A.9
.

Consider a Deligne finite category
{\mathcal{A}}
. Then

(1)

any Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{\mathcal{A}}
is bi-localising.

(2)

Any Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
and any quotient category
{\mathcal{A}}/{\mathcal{B}}
is Deligne finite.

Proof.

By Lemma
A.8
, a Serre subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of a Deligne finite category
{\mathcal{A}}
is the extension closure
{\mathcal{F}}({S_{i}})
of the simple objects in it.

Note that
{\mathcal{A}}
is equivalent to the category of right modules over a finite dimensional algebra
R
. A choice of idempotents
e_{i}
such that
e_{i}R
is a projective cover of
S_{i}
yields an equivalence of the Serre category generated by simple objects
\{S_{j}\}_{j\in J}
with
\textrm{mod-}R/RfR
, where
f=\sum_{i\notin J}e_{i}
. In particular,
{\mathcal{B}}
is Deligne finite.

Then the quotient category is
\textrm{mod-}fRf
, in particular it is Deligne finite. The quotient functor
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{R}(fR,-)\simeq(-)\otimes_{R}Rf\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to\textrm{mod-}fRf
has the left
(-)\otimes_{fRf}fR
and the right
\mathop{\textrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{fRf}(Rf,-)
adjoint.
∎
